and invoking the union bound over all the
|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|
entries
of
\bm{\xi}_{t}
demonstrate that

\displaystyle|\bm{\xi}_{t}|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{8\Big(\bm{W}_{t}+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2^{K}}\bm{1}\Big)\log\frac{8|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T\log\frac{1}{1-\gamma}}{\delta}}+\Big(\frac{4}{3}B\log\frac{8|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T\log\frac{1}{1-\gamma}}{\delta}\Big)\cdot\bm{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{16\Big(\bm{W}_{t}+\frac{2\log^{4}T}{(1-\gamma)T}\bm{1}\Big)\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}}+\Big(3B\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\Big)\cdot\bm{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{32\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)T}\Big(\max_{(1-\beta)t\leq i<t}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}(\bm{V}_{i})+\bm{1}\Big)}+\frac{12\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)^{2}T}\bm{1}

with probability at least
1-\delta/T.
Here, the second line holds
due to (
74
) and the fact
\log\frac{8|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T\log\frac{1}{1-\gamma}}{\delta}\leq 2\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}

(cf. (
65
)), whereas the last inequality makes
use of the relation (
73
).

Step 3: using the bounds on
\bm{\zeta}_{t}
and
\bm{\xi}_{t}

to control
\bm{\Delta}_{t}
.

Let us define

\bm{\varphi}_{t}\coloneqq 64\frac{\log^{4}T\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}}{(1-\gamma)T}\bigg(\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i\leq t}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}(\bm{V}_{i})+\bm{1}\bigg)

(75)

In view of the upper bounds derived in Steps 1 and 2, and
\beta
defined in (
68
),
we have—with probability exceeding

1-\delta
—that

|\bm{\zeta}_{k}|+|\bm{\xi}_{k}|\leq\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}\qquad\text{for all }\penalty\ 2t/3\leq k\leq t,

(76)

provided that
T\geq\frac{c_{9}(\log^{4}T)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}
for some sufficiently large constant
c_{9}>0
.
Substituting (
76
) into (
41
), we
can upper bound
\bm{\Delta}_{t}
as follows

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}+\sum_{i=(1-\beta)k+1}^{k}\eta_{i}^{(k)}\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i-1}}\bm{\Delta}_{i-1}=\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}+\sum_{i=(1-\beta)k}^{k-1}\eta_{i+1}^{(k)}\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i}}\bm{\Delta}_{i}\qquad\text{for all }\penalty\ 2t/3\leq k\leq t.

(77)

Further, we find it convenient to define
\big\{\alpha_{i}^{(t)}\big\}

as follows

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}^{(t)}:=\frac{\eta_{i+1}^{(t)}}{\sum_{j=(1-\beta)t}^{t-1}\eta_{j+1}^{(t)}}.

(78)

Clearly, this sequence satisfies

\alpha_{i}^{(t)}\geq\eta_{i+1}^{(t)}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\sum_{i=(1-\beta)t}^{t-1}\alpha_{i}^{(t)}=1

(79)

for any
t
, where the first inequality results from (
71
).
With these in place, we can write (
77
) as

\bm{\Delta}_{k}\leq\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}+\sum_{i_{1}=(1-\beta)k}^{k-1}\eta_{i_{1}+1}^{(k)}\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{1}}}\bm{\Delta}_{i_{1}}=\sum_{i_{1}=(1-\beta)k}^{k-1}\Big(\alpha_{i_{1}}^{(k)}\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}+\eta_{i_{1}+1}^{(k)}\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{1}}}\bm{\Delta}_{i_{1}}\Big)\qquad\text{for all }2t/3\leq k\leq t.

(80)

Given that
(1-\beta)t\geq 2t/3
(see (
68
)), we
can invoke this relation recursively to yield

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i_{1}=(1-\beta)t}^{t-1}\Big(\alpha_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}+\eta_{i_{1}+1}^{(t)}\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{1}}}\bm{\Delta}_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i_{1}=(1-\beta)t}^{t-1}\Bigg[\alpha_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}+\eta_{i_{1}+1}^{(t)}\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{1}}}\sum_{i_{2}=(1-\beta)i_{1}}^{i_{1}-1}\Big(\alpha_{i_{2}}^{(i_{1})}\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}+\eta_{i_{2}+1}^{(i_{1})}\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{2}}}\bm{\Delta}_{i_{2}}\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i_{1}=(1-\beta)t}^{t-1}\alpha_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}+\sum_{i_{1}=(1-\beta)t}^{t-1}\sum_{i_{2}=(1-\beta)i_{1}}^{i_{1}-1}\alpha_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\alpha_{i_{2}}^{(i_{1})}\big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{1}}}\big)\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{i_{1}=(1-\beta)t}^{t-1}\sum_{i_{2}=(1-\beta)i_{1}}^{i_{1}-1}\eta_{i_{1}+1}^{(t)}\eta_{i_{2}+1}^{(i_{1})}\prod_{k=1}^{2}\big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\big)\bm{\Delta}_{i_{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}=(1-\beta)t}^{t-1}\sum_{i_{2}=(1-\beta)i_{1}}^{i_{1}-1}\alpha_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\alpha_{i_{2}}^{(i_{1})}\left\{\bm{I}+\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{1}}}\right\}\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}+\sum_{i_{1}=(1-\beta)t}^{t-1}\sum_{i_{2}=(1-\beta)i_{1}}^{i_{1}-1}\eta_{i_{1}+1}^{(t)}\eta_{i_{2}+1}^{(i_{1})}\prod_{k=1}^{2}\big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\big)\bm{\Delta}_{i_{2}},

(81)

where the second inequality relies on (
80
), the third line uses the inequality
\eta_{i_{1}+1}^{(t)}\leq\alpha_{i_{1}}^{(t)}
in (
79
), and the fourth line is valid
since
\sum_{i_{2}=(1-\beta)i_{1}}^{i_{1}-1}\alpha_{i_{2}}^{(i_{1})}=1

(see (
79
)).

We intend to continue invoking (
80
)
recursively—similar to how we derive (
81
)—in
order to control
\bm{\Delta}_{t}
. To do so, we are in need of some
preparation. First, let us define

H\coloneqq\frac{\log T}{1-\gamma}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\alpha_{\{i_{k}\}_{k=1}^{H}}:=\alpha_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\alpha_{i_{2}}^{(i_{1})}\ldots\alpha_{i_{H}}^{(i_{H-1})}\geq 0

(82)

for any
t>i_{1}>i_{2}>\cdots>i_{H}
, which clearly satisfies (see
(
79
))

\alpha_{\{i_{k}\}_{k=1}^{H}}\geq\eta_{i_{1}+1}^{(t)}\eta_{i_{2}+1}^{(i_{1})}\ldots\eta_{i_{H}+1}^{(i_{H-1})}.

(83)

In addition, defining the index set

\mathcal{I}_{t}\coloneqq\Big\{\left(i_{1},\cdots,i_{H}\right)\,|\,(1-\beta)t\leq i_{1}\leq t-1,\ \forall 1\leq j<H:(1-\beta)i_{j}\leq i_{j+1}\leq i_{j}-1\Big\},

(84)

we have

\sum_{\left(i_{1},\cdots,i_{H}\right)\in\mathcal{I}_{t}}\alpha_{\{i_{k}\}_{k=1}^{H}}=1.

(85)

Additionally, recalling that
\beta=c_{4}(1-\gamma)/\log T
, we see
that this choice of
H
satisfies

(1-\beta)^{H}=\left(1-\frac{c_{4}(1-\gamma)}{\log T}\right)^{\frac{\log T}{1-\gamma}}\geq\frac{2}{3}

for
c_{4}
small enough, thus implying that

i_{1}>i_{2}>\cdots>i_{H}\geq(1-\beta)^{H}t\geq 2t/3\qquad\text{for all }(i_{1},\cdots,i_{H})\in\mathcal{I}_{t}.

This is an important property that allows one to invoke the relation
(
80
). With these in place, applying the
preceding relation (
80
) recursively—in a way similar to (
81
)—further leads to

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\left(i_{1},\cdots,i_{H}\right)\in\mathcal{I}_{t}}\alpha_{\{i_{k}\}_{k=1}^{H}}\Bigg\{\bigg(\bm{I}+\sum_{h=1}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bigg)\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}+\gamma^{H}\prod_{k=1}^{H}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\big|\bm{\Delta}_{i_{H}}\big|\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\left(i_{1},\cdots,i_{H}\right)\in\mathcal{I}_{t}}\Bigg\{\underbrace{\bigg(\bm{I}+\sum_{h=1}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bigg)\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}}_{=:\,\bm{\beta}_{1}}+\underbrace{\gamma^{H}\prod_{k=1}^{H}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\big|\bm{\Delta}_{i_{H}}\big|}_{=:\,\bm{\beta}_{2}}\Bigg\}

(86)

for all
t\geq\frac{T}{c_{2}\log T}
, where we recall the definition of the entrywise
\max
operator in Section
4.1
.
Here, the last inequality relies on the fact that
\sum_{\left(i_{1},\cdots,i_{H}\right)\in\mathcal{I}_{t}}\alpha_{\{i_{k}\}_{k=1}^{H}}=1
(see (
85
)).
It remains to control
\bm{\beta}_{1}
and
\bm{\beta}_{2}
, which we shall accomplish separately in the next two steps.

Step 4: bounding
\bm{\beta}_{2}
.

The term
\bm{\beta}_{2}
defined in (
86
) is relatively easier to control. Observing that
\prod_{k=1}^{H}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}

is still a probability transition matrix, we can derive

\displaystyle|\bm{\beta}_{2}|

\displaystyle=\gamma^{H}\prod_{1\leq k\leq h}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\big|\bm{\Delta}_{i_{H}}\big|\leq\gamma^{H}\bigg\|\prod_{1\leq k\leq h}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bigg\|_{1}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i_{H}}\|_{\infty}=\gamma^{H}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i_{H}}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{\leq}\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\gamma^{H}\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\leq}\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)T},

where (i) results from the crude bound (
64
).
To justify the inequality (ii), we recall the definition (
82
)
of
H
to see that

\gamma^{H}\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{=}\big(1-(1-\gamma)\big)^{\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\log T}\leq e^{-\log T}=\frac{1}{T},

where the inequality comes from the elementary fact that
\gamma^{\frac{1}{1-\gamma}}\leq e^{-1}

for any
0<\gamma<1
.

Step 5: bounding
\bm{\beta}_{1}
.

When it comes to the term
\bm{\beta}_{1}
defined in (
86
), we can upper bound the entrywise
square of
\bm{\beta}_{1}
—denoted by
|\bm{\beta}_{1}|^{2}
—as follows

\displaystyle|\bm{\beta}_{1}|^{2}

\displaystyle=\bigg|\Big(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\prod_{1\leq k\leq h}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\Big)\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}\bigg|^{2}\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{\leq}\bigg|\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h/2}\cdot\gamma^{h/2}\sqrt{\prod_{1\leq k\leq h}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bm{\varphi}_{t}}\,\bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\leq}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\cdot\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bm{\varphi}_{t}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{iii})}{\leq}\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\frac{64\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)T}\bigg(\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}(\bm{V}_{i})+\bm{1}\bigg)

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{iv})}{\leq}\frac{64\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)^{2}T}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}(\bm{V}_{i})+\frac{64\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)^{3}T}\bm{1}.

Here, (i) follows from Jensen’s inequality and the fact that
\prod_{k=1}^{h}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}

is a probability transition matrix; (ii) holds due to the Cauchy-Schwarz
inequality; (iii) utilizes the definition of
\bm{\varphi}_{t}
in
(
75
); (iv) follows since
\prod_{1\leq k\leq h}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bm{1}=\bm{1}

and
\sum_{0\leq h<H}\gamma^{h}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
.
To further control the right-hand side of the above inequality, we resort to the following lemma.

Lemma 5
.

Suppose that
t\geq\frac{T}{c_{2}\log T}
. For any
\left(i_{1},\cdots,i_{H}\right)\in\mathcal{I}_{t}
,
the following holds:

\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}(\bm{V}_{i})\leq\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{2}}\Big(1+2\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\Big)\bm{1}.

(87)

Proof.

This lemma, which is inspired by but significantly more complicated than
Azar et al., 2013
,
plays a key role in shaving one
\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
factor.
See Section
B.5
for the proof.∎

Therefore, the above result directly implies that

\displaystyle|\bm{\beta}_{1}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{320\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\Big(1+2\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\Big)\bm{1}.

(88)

Step 6: putting all this together.

Substituting the preceding bounds for
\bm{\beta}_{1}
and
\bm{\beta}_{2}

into (
86
), we can demonstrate that: with
probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)T}\bm{1}+\sqrt{\frac{320\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\Big(1+2\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\Big)}\ \bm{1}

\displaystyle\leq 30\sqrt{\frac{\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\Big(1+\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\Big)}\ \bm{1}

(89)

holds simultaneously for all
t\geq\frac{T}{c_{2}\log T}
,
where the second line is valid since
\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)T}\leq\sqrt{\frac{(\log^{4}T)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)^{4}T}}

under our sample size condition (
65
).

B.3
Proof of Lemma
2

Next, we move forward to develop an lower bound on
\bm{\Delta}_{t}
,
which can be accomplished in an analogous manner as for the above
upper bound. Applying a similar argument for (
86
)
(except that we need to replace
\pi_{i}
with
\pi^{\star}
), one
can deduce that

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{t}

\displaystyle\geq-\max_{\left(i_{1},\cdots,i_{H}\right)\in\mathcal{I}_{t}}\left\{\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\bm{P}^{\pi^{\star}}\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}+\gamma^{H}\prod_{k=1}^{H}\bm{P}^{\pi^{\star}}\big|\bm{\Delta}_{i_{H}}\big|\right\}

(90)

for any
t\geq\frac{c_{2}T}{\log\frac{1}{1-\gamma}}
. It is straightforward
to bound the second term on the right-hand side of (
90
)
as

\gamma^{H}\prod_{1\leq k\leq H}\bm{P}^{\pi^{\star}}\big|\bm{\Delta}_{i_{H}}\big|\leq\gamma^{H}\bigg\|\prod_{1\leq k\leq H}\bm{P}^{\pi^{\star}}\bigg\|_{1}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i_{H}}\|_{\infty}\bm{1}\leq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)T}\bm{1},

where the second inequality makes use of (
64
)
as well as the fact that
\prod_{k}\bm{P}^{\pi^{\star}}
is a probability
transition matrix (so that
\|\prod_{k}\bm{P}^{\pi^{\star}}\big\|_{1}=1
).
As for the first term on the right-hand side of (
90
),
we can invoke a similar argument for (
88
) to obtain

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\bm{P}^{\pi^{\star}}\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}\bigg|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 320\frac{\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\bigg(1+2\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\bigg)\bm{1}.

Taking these two bounds together, we see that with probability at
least
1-\delta
,

\bm{\Delta}_{t}\geq-30\sqrt{\frac{\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\bigg(1+\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\bigg)}\ \bm{1}

(91)

holds simultaneously for all
t\geq\frac{T}{c_{2}\log T}
.

B.4
Solving the recurrence relation regarding
\bm{\Delta}_{t}

Recall from (
42
) that with probability exceeding
1-2\delta
,
the following recurrence relation

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}_{t}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 30\sqrt{\frac{\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\bigg(1+\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\bigg)}\qquad\text{for all}\qquad t\geq\frac{T}{c_{2}\log T}

(92)

holds, which plays a crucial role
in establishing the desired estimation error bound. Specifically,
for any
k\geq 0
, let us define

u_{k}:=\max\left\{\|\bm{\Delta}_{t}\|_{\infty}\ \Big|\ 2^{k}\frac{T}{c_{2}\log T}\leq t\leq T\right\}.

(93)

To bound this sequence, we first obtain a crude bound as a result
of (
64
):

u_{0}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}.

(94)

Next, it is directly seen from (
92
)
and the definition of
u_{k}
that

u_{k}\leq c_{6}\sqrt{\frac{\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\left(1+u_{k-1}\right)},\qquad k\geq 1

(95)

for some constant
c_{6}=20/\gamma>0
. In order to analyze the size of
u_{k}
,
we divide into two cases.

•

If
u_{k}\leq 1
for some
k\geq 1
, then (
95
)
tells us that

u_{k+1}\leq c_{6}\sqrt{\frac{\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\left(1+u_{k}\right)}\leq c_{6}\sqrt{\frac{2\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}}\leq 1,

as long as
T\geq\frac{2c_{6}^{2}\log^{4}T\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}}
.
In other words, once
u_{k-1}
drops below
1
, then all subsequent
quantities will remain bounded above by 1, namely,
\max_{j:j\geq k}u_{j}\leq 1
.
As a result,

u_{j}\leq c_{6}\sqrt{\frac{\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\left(1+u_{j-1}\right)}\leq c_{6}\sqrt{\frac{2\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}}\qquad\text{for all }j>k.

•

Instead, suppose that
u_{j}>1
for all
0\leq j\leq k
. Then it
is seen from (
95
) that

u_{j+1}\leq c_{6}\sqrt{\frac{\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\left(1+u_{j}\right)}\leq c_{6}\sqrt{\frac{2\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}u_{j}}\qquad\text{for all }j\leq k.

This is equivalent to saying that

\log u_{j+1}\leq\log\alpha_{u}+\frac{1}{2}\log u_{j}\qquad\text{for all }j\leq k,

where
\alpha_{u}=c_{6}\sqrt{\frac{2\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}}.

Invoking a standard analysis strategy for this type of recursive relations
yields

\log u_{j+1}-2\log\alpha_{u}\leq\frac{1}{2}\left(\log u_{j}-2\log\alpha_{u}\right)\qquad\text{for all }j\leq k,

and hence

\log u_{j+1}\leq 2\log\alpha_{u}+\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{j+1}\left(\log u_{0}-2\log\alpha_{u}\right)\qquad\text{for all }j\leq k.

This is equivalent to saying that

u_{j}\leq\alpha_{u}^{2}\left(\frac{u_{0}}{\alpha_{u}^{2}}\right)^{1/2^{j}}=\left(\alpha_{u}^{2}\right)^{1-1/2^{j}}\left(u_{0}\right)^{1/2^{j}}\qquad\text{for all }j\leq k+1.

Putting the above two cases together and using (
94
), we conclude that

\displaystyle u_{k}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{2c_{6}^{2}\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}}+\Big(\frac{2c_{6}^{2}\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\Big)^{1-1/2^{k}}u_{0}^{1/2^{k}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{2c_{6}^{2}\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}}+\Big(\frac{2c_{6}^{2}\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\Big)^{1-1/2^{k}}\left(\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\right)^{1/2^{k}},\qquad k\geq 1.

In particular, as long as
k\geq c_{7}\log\log\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
for some constant
c_{7}>0
, one
has
\big(\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\big)^{1/2^{k}}\leq O(1)
and

\Big(\frac{2c_{6}^{2}\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\Big)^{1-1/2^{k}}\leq\max\left\{\sqrt{\frac{2c_{6}^{2}\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}},\frac{2c_{6}^{2}\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\right\}.

As a result, the above bound simplifies to

\displaystyle u_{k}

\displaystyle\leq c_{8}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}}+\frac{\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\bigg),\qquad k\geq c_{7}\log\log\frac{1}{1-\gamma}

for some constant
c_{8}>0
.

Consequently, taking
t=T
and choosing
k=c_{7}\log\log\frac{1}{1-\gamma}

for some appropriate constant
c_{7}>0
(so as to ensure
2^{k}\frac{T}{c_{2}\log T}<T
),
we immediately see from the definition (
93
)
of
u_{k}
that

\|\bm{\Delta}_{T}\|_{\infty}\leq c_{8}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}}+\frac{\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\bigg).

(96)

with probability at least
1-2\delta
. To finish up, we note that
the sample size assumption (
61
)
is equivalent to

\frac{\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\leq\frac{\min\{\varepsilon^{2},\varepsilon\}}{c_{3}}.

When
c_{3}>0
is sufficiently large, substituting this relation
into (
96
) gives

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}_{T}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\min\{\varepsilon^{2},\varepsilon\}}+\frac{1}{2}\min\{\varepsilon^{2},\varepsilon\}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon+\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{2}&\text{if }\varepsilon\leq 1\\
\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon\quad&\text{if }\varepsilon>1\end{cases}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon

as claimed in Theorem
7
.

B.5
Proof of Lemma
5

We first claim that

\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}(\bm{V}_{i})-\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}(\bm{V}^{\star})\leq\frac{4}{1-\gamma}\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\bm{1}.

(97)

If this claim were valid (which we shall justify towards the end of
this subsection), then it would lead to

\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}(\bm{V}_{i})\leq\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}(\bm{V}^{\star})+\frac{4}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\bm{1}.

(98)

It then boils down to bounding the first term on the right-hand side
of (
98
).
Let us first upper bound the variance term involving
\bm{V}^{\star}
.
For any
0\leq h<H
, one can express (see (
26
))

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}(\bm{V}^{\star})

\displaystyle=\bm{P}(\bm{V}^{\star}\circ\bm{V}^{\star})-(\bm{P}\bm{V}^{\star})\circ(\bm{P}\bm{V}^{\star})

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{=}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{h+1}}}(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star})+\bm{P}(\bm{V}^{\star}\circ\bm{V}^{\star})-\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{h+1}}}(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star})-\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}(\bm{Q}^{\star}-\bm{r})\circ(\bm{Q}^{\star}-\bm{r})

\displaystyle=\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{h+1}}}(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star})+\bm{P}^{\pi^{\star}}(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star})-\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{h+1}}}(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star})-\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}(\bm{Q}^{\star}-\bm{r})\circ(\bm{Q}^{\star}-\bm{r})

\displaystyle\leq\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{h+1}}}(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star})+\big\|\bm{P}^{\pi^{\star}}(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star})-\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{h+1}}}(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star})\big\|_{\infty}\bm{1}-\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}(\bm{Q}^{\star}-\bm{r})\circ(\bm{Q}^{\star}-\bm{r})

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\leq}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{h+1}}}(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star})+\frac{4}{1-\gamma}\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\bm{1}-\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}(\bm{Q}^{\star}-\bm{r})\circ(\bm{Q}^{\star}-\bm{r})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\big(\gamma^{2}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{h+1}}}(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star})-\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star}\big)+\frac{4}{1-\gamma}\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\bm{1}-\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\bm{r}\circ\bm{r}+\frac{2}{\gamma^{2}}\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{r}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{iii})}{\leq}\frac{1}{\gamma}\big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{h+1}}}(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star})-\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star}\big)+\frac{2}{\gamma^{2}}\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{r}+\frac{4}{1-\gamma}\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\bm{1},

(99)

where (i) relies on the identity
\bm{Q}^{\star}=\bm{r}+\gamma\bm{P}\bm{V}^{\star}
,
and (iii) holds since
0<\gamma<1
. To justify (ii), we make the
following observation:

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{h+1}}}\big(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star}\big)-\bm{P}^{\pi^{\star}}\big(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star}\big)\big\|_{\infty}=\big\|\bm{P}\bm{\Pi}^{\pi_{i_{h+1}}}\big(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star}\big)-\bm{P}\bm{\Pi}^{\pi^{\star}}\big(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star}\big)\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\overset{(\mathrm{iv})}{\leq}\big\|\bm{\Pi}^{\pi_{i_{h+1}}}\big(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star}\big)-\bm{\Pi}^{\pi^{\star}}\big(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star}\big)\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad=\big\|\big(\bm{\Pi}^{\pi_{i_{h+1}}}\bm{Q}^{\star}-\bm{\Pi}^{\pi^{\star}}\bm{Q}^{\star}\big)\circ\big(\bm{\Pi}^{\pi_{i_{h+1}}}\bm{Q}^{\star}+\bm{\Pi}^{\pi^{\star}}\bm{Q}^{\star}\big)\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\overset{(\mathrm{v})}{\leq}\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\big\|\bm{\Pi}^{\pi_{i_{h+1}}}\bm{Q}^{\star}-\bm{\Pi}^{\pi^{\star}}\bm{Q}^{\star}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\Big(\big\|\bm{\Pi}^{\pi_{i_{h+1}}}\bm{Q}^{\star}-\bm{\Pi}^{\pi_{i_{h+1}}}\bm{Q}_{i_{h+1}}\big\|_{\infty}+\big\|\bm{\Pi}^{\pi_{i_{h+1}}}\bm{Q}_{i_{h+1}}-\bm{V}^{\star}\big\|_{\infty}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\overset{\mathrm{(vi)}}{\leq}\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\Big(\big\|\bm{Q}^{\star}-\bm{Q}_{i_{h+1}}\big\|_{\infty}+\big\|\bm{V}_{i_{h+1}}-\bm{V}^{\star}\big\|_{\infty}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\overset{\mathrm{(vii)}}{\leq}\frac{4}{1-\gamma}\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty},

where (iv) arises from the fact
\|\bm{P}\bm{z}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\bm{P}\|_{1}\|\bm{z}\|_{\infty}=\|\bm{z}\|_{\infty}
,
(v) is valid because
\|\bm{Q}^{\star}\|_{\infty}\leq 1/(1-\gamma)
, (vi) follows from the fact that
\bm{V}_{i_{h+1}}=\bm{\Pi}^{\pi_{i_{h+1}}}\bm{Q}_{i_{h+1}}
,
and (vi) holds since
\|\bm{V}_{i_{h+1}}-\bm{V}^{\star}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\bm{Q}_{i_{h+1}}-\bm{Q}^{\star}\|_{\infty}
.

As it turns out, the first term in (
99
) allows one to build a telescoping sum. Specifically, invoking (
99
) allows one to bound

\displaystyle\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}(\bm{V}^{\star})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\gamma}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{h+1}}}(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star})-\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{4}{1-\gamma}\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bm{1}+\frac{2}{\gamma^{2}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\left(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{r}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{=}\frac{1}{\gamma}\Big(\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\prod_{k=1}^{h+1}\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}-\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\Big)\left(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{4}{1-\gamma}\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\bm{1}+\frac{2}{\gamma^{2}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\left(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{r}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\gamma}\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{H}\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}-\bm{I}\bigg)\left(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{Q}^{\star}\right)+\frac{4}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\bm{1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{2}{\gamma^{2}}\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\left(\bm{Q}^{\star}\circ\bm{r}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\leq}\bigg(\frac{2}{\gamma}\|\bm{Q}^{\star}\|_{\infty}^{2}+\frac{4}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}+\frac{2}{\gamma^{2}}\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\|\bm{Q}^{\star}\|_{\infty}\|\bm{r}\|_{\infty}\bigg)\bm{1}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{iii})}{\leq}\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\bigg(\frac{2}{\gamma}+4\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}+\frac{2}{\gamma^{2}}\bigg)\bm{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\bigg(\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}}+4\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\bigg)\bm{1}.

(100)

Here, (i) comes from the identity
\prod_{k=1}^{h}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bm{1}=\bm{1}
;
(ii) holds because each row of
\prod_{k=1}^{h}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}

has unit
\|\cdot\|_{1}
norm for any
h
; (iii) arises from the
bound
\|\bm{Q}^{\star}\|_{\infty}\leq 1/(1-\gamma)
. This completes
the proof, as long as the claim (
97
)
can be justified.

Proof of the inequality (
97
).

To validate this result, we make the observation that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}(\bm{V}_{i})-\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}(\bm{V}^{\star})

\displaystyle=\big[\bm{P}(\bm{V}_{i}\circ\bm{V}_{i})-(\bm{P}\bm{V}_{i})\circ(\bm{P}\bm{V}_{i})\big]-\big[\bm{P}(\bm{V}^{\star}\circ\bm{V}^{\star})-(\bm{P}\bm{V}^{\star})\circ(\bm{P}\bm{V}^{\star})\big]

\displaystyle=\bm{P}(\bm{V}_{i}\circ\bm{V}_{i}-\bm{V}^{\star}\circ\bm{V}^{\star})+(\bm{P}\bm{V}^{\star})\circ(\bm{P}\bm{V}^{\star})-(\bm{P}\bm{V}_{i})\circ(\bm{P}\bm{V}_{i})

\displaystyle=\bm{P}\big((\bm{V}_{i}-\bm{V}^{\star})\circ(\bm{V}_{i}+\bm{V}^{\star})\big)+(\bm{P}\bm{V}^{\star}-\bm{P}\bm{V}_{i})\circ(\bm{P}\bm{V}^{\star}+\bm{P}\bm{V}_{i})

\displaystyle\leq\Big\{\big\|\bm{P}\big((\bm{V}_{i}-\bm{V}^{\star})\circ(\bm{V}_{i}+\bm{V}^{\star})\big)\big\|_{\infty}+\big\|(\bm{P}\bm{V}^{\star}-\bm{P}\bm{V}_{i})\circ(\bm{P}\bm{V}^{\star}+\bm{P}\bm{V}_{i})\big\|_{\infty}\Big\}\bm{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{1-\gamma}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\bm{1}.

Here, the last inequality follows since (by applying Lemma
4
)

\big\|\bm{P}\big((\bm{V}_{i}-\bm{V}^{\star})\circ(\bm{V}_{i}+\bm{V}^{\star})\big)\big\|_{\infty}\leq\|\bm{P}\|_{1}\|\bm{V}_{i}-\bm{V}^{\star}\|_{\infty}\|\bm{V}_{i}+\bm{V}^{\star}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty},

\text{and}\quad\big\|(\bm{P}\bm{V}^{\star}-\bm{P}\bm{V}_{i})\circ(\bm{P}\bm{V}^{\star}+\bm{P}\bm{V}_{i})\big\|_{\infty}\leq\|\bm{P}\|_{1}\|\bm{V}_{i}-\bm{V}^{\star}\|_{\infty}\cdot\|\bm{P}\|_{1}\|\bm{V}_{i}+\bm{V}^{\star}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}.

A useful extension of Lemma
5
.

Before concluding, we make note of the following extension that proves useful for studying asynchronous Q-learning.

Lemma 6
.

Suppose that
t\geq\frac{T}{c_{2}\log T}
. Then one has

\sum_{h=0}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\bm{P}^{\widehat{\pi}_{k}}\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}(\bm{V}_{i})\leq\frac{4}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{2}}\Big(1+2\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\Big)\bm{1}

(101)

for any set of policies
\{\widehat{\pi}_{k}\}
obeying
\{\widehat{\pi}_{k}\}\subseteq\Pi
. Here, we define

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Pi\coloneqq\big\{\pi=[\pi(s)]_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\,\big|\,\pi(s)\in\Pi_{s},\forall s\in\mathcal{S}\big\},\qquad\Pi_{s}\coloneqq\big\{\pi_{i}(s)\mid i\in[t/2,t)\big\}.\end{aligned}

(102)

The key difference between Lemma
6
and Lemma
5
is that:
the components of
\widehat{\pi}_{k}
corresponding to different states can be chosen in a separate manner.
The proof follows from an identical argument as the above proof of Lemma
5
, and is hence omitted.

Appendix C
Analysis for TD learning (Theorem
1
)

As it turns out, if
|\mathcal{A}|=1
(which reduces to the case of TD learning), we can further modify the previous analysis in Section
B
to yield an improved
\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}
scaling. This forms the main content of this section, which leads to the proof of Theorem
1
for TD learning. Akin to the Q-learning case, we proceed to establish a more general version of Theorem
1
that covers the full
\varepsilon
-range. This is formally stated below, which subsumes Theorem
1
as a special case.

Theorem 8
.

Consider any
\gamma\in(0,1)
and any
\varepsilon\in\big(0,\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\big]
. Theorem
1
continues to hold if

T\geq\frac{c_{3}\big(\log^{3}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{3}\min\{\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{2}\}}

(103)

for some sufficiently large universal constant
c_{3}>0
.

C.1
Preliminary facts

Before embarking on the analysis, we begin by presenting several useful preliminary facts.
The first one is a direct
consequence of the claimed iteration complexity (
103
)
when
\varepsilon\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
:

T\geq\frac{c_{3}\big(\log^{3}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{3}\min\{\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{2}\}}\geq\frac{c_{3}\big(\log^{3}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{2}},

(104)

a simple fact that will be used multiple times.
In addition, the update rule (
7
) of TD learning can be expressed using vector/matrix notation as follows

\displaystyle\bm{V}_{t}=(1-\eta_{t})\bm{V}_{t-1}+\eta_{t}(\bm{r}+\gamma\bm{P}_{t}\bm{V}_{t-1})\qquad\text{for all }t\geq 1,

(105)

where the matrix
\bm{P}_{t}\in\{0,1\}^{|\mathcal{S}|\times|\mathcal{S}|}
obeys

\bm{P}_{t}(s,s^{\prime})\coloneqq\begin{cases}1,\quad&\text{if }s^{\prime}=s_{t}(s)\\
0,&\text{else}\end{cases}

for any
s,s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
.
In the sequel, we collect a few other facts concerning the range of
\bm{V}_{t}
and learning rates.

Range of
\bm{V}_{t}
.

We claim that: when the initialization
\bm{V}_{0}
obeys
\bm{0}\leq\bm{V}_{0}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\bm{1}
,
the TD learning iterates obey

\displaystyle\bm{0}\leq\bm{V}_{t}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\bm{1}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\|\bm{V}_{t}-\bm{V}^{\star}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\qquad\text{for all }t\geq 0,

(106)

provided that
0\leq\eta_{t}\leq 1
for all
t\geq 0
. The proof follows immediately by repeating the proof of Lemma
4
(see Section
B.1
) with
|\mathcal{A}|=1
, and is hence omitted for brevity.

Learning rates.

We shall also collect several useful results concerning the learning rates
\{\eta_{t}\}
.
Let us abuse the notation by defining the following crucial quantities:

\eta_{k}^{(t)}\coloneqq\begin{cases}\prod_{i=1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big),&\text{if }k=0,\\
\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big),&\text{if }0<k<t,\\
\eta_{t},&\text{if }k=t.\end{cases}

(107)

Note that this definition (
107
) differs from the one (
66
) used for Q-learning, and will only be employed in this section.
Consider any iteration number
t
satisfying

t\geq\frac{T}{c_{2}\log T}.

(108)

Clearly, the learning rate
\eta_{t}
under Assumption (
8a
) obeys

(1-\gamma)\eta_{t}\geq\frac{1-\gamma}{1+\frac{c_{1}(1-\gamma)T}{\log^{2}T}}\geq\frac{1-\gamma}{\frac{2c_{1}(1-\gamma)T}{\log^{2}T}}=\frac{\log^{2}T}{2c_{1}T}.

(109)

In what follows, we intend to bound
\eta_{k}^{(t)}
for two cases separately.

•

For any
i
obeying
0\leq i\leq t/2
, it is easily seen from (
109
) that

\displaystyle\eta_{i}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\leq\big(1-\eta_{t/2}(1-\gamma)\big)^{t/2}\leq\Big(1-\frac{\log^{2}T}{2c_{1}T}\Big)^{t/2}\leq\Big(1-\frac{\log^{2}T}{2c_{1}T}\Big)^{\frac{T}{2c_{2}\log T}}

\displaystyle=\left\{\Big(1-\frac{\log^{2}T}{2c_{1}T}\Big)^{\frac{2c_{1}T}{\log^{2}T}}\right\}^{\frac{\log T}{4c_{1}c_{2}}}\leq\frac{1}{T^{2}},

(110a)

where the last inequality holds as long as
c_{1}c_{2}\leq 1/8
and (
104
) holds.

•

When it comes to the case with
i>t/2
, we can develop the following upper bound

\eta_{i}^{(t)}\leq\eta_{i}\leq\frac{1}{c_{2}(1-\gamma)i/\log^{2}T}<\frac{2\log^{3}T}{(1-\gamma)T},

(110b)

which relies on Assumption (
8a
).

In addition,
given that
\bm{P}^{k}\bm{1}=\bm{1}
for any integer
k>0
, it can be easily verified that

\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)\bm{1}=\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big)\bm{1},

and as a result,

\displaystyle\Bigg\|\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)\Bigg\|_{1}=\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big).

(111)

C.2
Proof of Theorem
8

Step 1: decomposing the error
\bm{V}_{t}-\bm{V}^{\star}
.

Taking
\bm{\Delta}_{t}\coloneqq\bm{V}_{t}-\bm{V}^{\star}
, via the basic relation (
32
), the TD learning update rule can be written as

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{t}

\displaystyle=(1-\eta_{t})\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}+\eta_{t}\gamma\big(\bm{P}\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}+(\bm{P}_{t}-\bm{P})\bm{V}_{t-1}\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{t}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}+\eta_{t}\gamma(\bm{P}_{t}-\bm{P})\bm{V}_{t-1}.

(112)

Invoking the above relation recursively then leads to

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{t}=\prod_{i=1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)\bm{\Delta}_{0}+\underbrace{\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)\gamma(\bm{P}_{k}-\bm{P})\bm{V}_{k-1}}_{\eqqcolon\,\bm{\xi}_{t}}.

(113)

Step 2: controlling the first term of (
113
).

With regards to the first term of (
113
), we make the observation that

\displaystyle\Big\|\prod_{i=1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)\bm{\Delta}_{0}\Big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\prod_{i=1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)\Big\|_{1}\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{0}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\left\{\prod_{i=1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big)\right\}\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{0}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\eta_{0}^{(t)}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\leq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)T^{2}},

(114)

where the second line arises from (
111
),
and the last inequality holds true due to (
110a
) as long as
t\geq\frac{T}{c_{2}\log T}.

Step 3: controlling the second term of (
113
).

We then move on to the second term
\bm{\xi}_{t}
in (
113
), which admits the following expression

\displaystyle\bm{\xi}_{t}=\sum_{k=1}^{t}\bm{z}_{k}\qquad\text{with }\bm{z}_{k}\coloneqq\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)\gamma(\bm{P}_{k}-\bm{P})\bm{V}_{k-1}.

(115)

Here, the summands
\{\bm{z}_{k}\}
clearly satisfy

\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{z}_{k}\,|\,\bm{V}_{k-1},\cdots,\bm{V}_{0}\big]=\bm{0}.

We then attempt to invoke the Freedman inequality (see Theorem
6
)
to control this term.
Towards this end, there are several quantities that need to be calculated.

•

First of all, we observe that

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle\coloneqq\max_{1\leq k\leq t}\|\bm{z}_{k}\|_{\infty}\leq\max_{1\leq k\leq t}\Big\|\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)\gamma(\bm{P}_{k}-\bm{P})\bm{V}_{k-1}\Big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq k\leq t}\Big\|\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)\Big\|_{1}\big\|(\bm{P}_{k}-\bm{P})\bm{V}_{k-1}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\max_{1\leq k\leq t}\left\{\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big)\right\}\big\|(\bm{P}_{k}-\bm{P})\bm{V}_{k-1}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq k\leq t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}\big(\|\bm{P}_{k}\|_{1}+\|\bm{P}\|_{1}\big)\|\bm{V}_{k-1}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{4\log^{3}T}{(1-\gamma)^{2}T},

(116)

where the third line again makes use of the relation (
111
)
and the last line follows the facts
\|\bm{P}_{k}\|_{1}=\|\bm{P}\|_{1}=1
,
\|\bm{V}_{k-1}\|_{\infty}\leq 1/(1-\gamma)
,
as well as the properties (
110
).

•

The next step is to control certain variance terms.
Towards this, we first make note of a userful fact.
For any given non-negative vector
\bm{u}=[u_{i}]_{1\leq i\leq|\mathcal{S}|}\geq\bm{0}

and any vector
\bm{v}
, it is easily seen that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}\Big(\bm{u}^{\top}(\bm{P}_{k}-\bm{P})\bm{v}\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{|\mathcal{S}|}u_{i}^{2}\mathsf{Var}\Big((\bm{P}_{k}-\bm{P})_{i,\cdot}\bm{v}\Big)\leq\Big\{\max_{i}|u_{i}|\Big\}\big[u_{1},\cdots,u_{|\mathcal{S}|}\big]\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}\left(\bm{v}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{u}\|_{1}\bm{u}^{\top}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}\left(\bm{v}\right),

(117)

where we remind the reader of the notation
\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}(\bm{v})
in (
26
).
Additionally,
for any vector
\bm{a}=[a_{j}]
, let us employ the notation
\mathsf{Var}\big(\bm{a}\,|\,\bm{V}_{k-1},\cdots,\bm{V}_{0}\big)

to represent a vector whose
j
-th entry is given by
\mathsf{Var}\big(a_{j}\,|\,\bm{V}_{k-1},\cdots,\bm{V}_{0}\big)
.
Armed with this notation, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}\big(\bm{z}_{k}\,|\,\bm{V}_{k-1},\cdots,\bm{V}_{0}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\gamma^{2}\Big\|\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)\Big\|_{1}\left\{\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)\right\}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}\left(\bm{V}_{k-1}\right)

\displaystyle=\gamma^{2}\left\{\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big)\right\}\left\{\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)\right\}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}\left(\bm{V}_{k-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\eta_{k}\eta_{k}^{(t)}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}\left(\bm{V}_{k-1}\right),

(118)

where the first inequality is a consequence of (
117
) and the definition of
\bm{z}_{k}
(cf. (
115
)), the second line arises from (
111
), and the last relation results from the definition of
\eta_{k}^{(t)}
.
This in turn allows us to compute

\displaystyle\bm{W}_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{k=1}^{t}\mathsf{Var}\big(\bm{z}_{k}\,|\,\bm{V}_{k-1},\cdots,\bm{V}_{0}\big)\leq\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}\eta_{k}^{(t)}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}\left(\bm{V}_{k-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{t/2}\eta_{k}^{(t)}\Big\|\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)\Big\|_{1}\|\bm{V}_{k-1}\|_{\infty}^{2}\bm{1}+\sum_{k=t/2+1}^{t}\eta_{k}\eta_{k}^{(t)}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}\left(\bm{V}_{k-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{t/2}\big(\eta_{k}^{(t)}\big)^{2}\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\bm{1}+\Big\{\max_{k:\,t/2<k\leq t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}\Big\}\sum_{k=t/2+1}^{t}\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}\left(\bm{V}_{k-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2(1-\gamma)^{2}T^{3}}\bm{1}+\frac{2\log^{3}T}{(1-\gamma)T}\Bigg(\sum_{k=t/2+1}^{t}\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)\Bigg)\max_{k:\,t/2\leq k<t}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}\big(\bm{V}_{k}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2(1-\gamma)^{2}T^{3}}\bm{1}+\frac{2\log^{3}T}{(1-\gamma)T}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})^{-1}\max_{k:\,t/2\leq k<t}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}\big(\bm{V}_{k}\big),

(119)

where the penultimate inequality results from (
110
);
to see why the last inequality holds, observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=t/2+1}^{t}\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})^{-1}\sum_{k=t/2+1}^{t}\eta_{k}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})^{-1}\sum_{k=t/2+1}^{t}\bigg[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)-\prod_{i=k}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})^{-1}-(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})^{-1}\prod_{i=t/2+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})\big)\leq(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})^{-1},

where we have used the fact that all entries of
(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})^{-1}
and
\bm{I}-\eta_{i}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})
are non-negative.

•

In addition, we also derive the following
trivial upper bound based on (
119
):

\displaystyle\big|\bm{W}_{t}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2(1-\gamma)^{2}T^{3}}\bm{1}+\frac{2\log^{3}T}{(1-\gamma)T}\big\|(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})^{-1}\big\|_{1}\max_{k:\,t/2\leq k<t}\big\|\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}\big(\bm{V}_{k}\big)\big\|_{\infty}\bm{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2(1-\gamma)^{2}T^{3}}\bm{1}+\frac{2\log^{3}T}{(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\bm{1}\leq\frac{3\log^{3}T}{(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\bm{1}\eqqcolon\sigma^{2}\bm{1},

(120)

where we have invoked the fact that
\|(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})^{-1}\|_{1}=1/(1-\gamma)
.
Therefore, by setting
K=\big\lceil 2\log_{2}\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\big\rceil
, one arrives at

\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2^{K}}\leq\frac{3\log^{3}T}{(1-\gamma)^{2}T}.

(121)

Equipped with the preceding bounds, let us apply the Freedman inequality in
Theorem
6
and invoke the union bound over all entries
of
\bm{\xi}_{t}
to show that

\displaystyle|\bm{\xi}_{t}|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{8\Big(\bm{W}_{t}+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2^{K}}\bm{1}\Big)\log\frac{8|\mathcal{S}|T\log\frac{1}{1-\gamma}}{\delta}}+\Big(\frac{4}{3}B\log\frac{8|\mathcal{S}|T\log\frac{1}{1-\gamma}}{\delta}\Big)\bm{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{16\Big(\bm{W}_{t}+\frac{3\log^{3}T}{(1-\gamma)^{2}T}\bm{1}\Big)\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}|T}{\delta}}+\Big(3B\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}|T}{\delta}\Big)\bm{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{32\big(\log^{3}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)T}\Big((\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})^{-1}\max_{k:\,t/2\leq k<t}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}\big(\bm{V}_{k}\big)+\frac{2}{1-\gamma}\bm{1}\Big)}+\frac{12\big(\log^{3}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)^{2}T}\bm{1}

with probability at least
1-\delta/T.

Here, the second line follows since

\log\frac{8|\mathcal{S}|T\log\frac{1}{1-\gamma}}{\delta}\leq 2\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}|T}{\delta}

as long as
\frac{|\mathcal{S}|T}{\delta}\geq 8\log\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
,
whereas the last line holds by using (
116
), (
119
) and (
121
).
Further, we make the observation that

\displaystyle(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})^{-1}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}\big(\bm{V}^{\star}\big)

\displaystyle=(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})^{-1}\Big(\bm{P}(\bm{V}^{\star}\circ\bm{V}^{\star})-(\bm{P}\bm{V}^{\star})\circ(\bm{P}\bm{V}^{\star})\Big)

\displaystyle=(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})^{-1}\left(\bm{P}(\bm{V}^{\star}\circ\bm{V}^{\star})-\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}(\bm{V}^{\star}-\bm{r})\circ(\bm{V}^{\star}-\bm{r})\right)

\displaystyle\leq(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})^{-1}\left(\bm{P}(\bm{V}^{\star}\circ\bm{V}^{\star})-\frac{1}{\gamma^{2}}\bm{V}^{\star}\circ\bm{V}^{\star}+\frac{2}{\gamma^{2}}\bm{r}\circ\bm{V}^{\star}\right)

\displaystyle\leq(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})^{-1}\left(\bm{P}(\bm{V}^{\star}\circ\bm{V}^{\star})-\frac{1}{\gamma}\bm{V}^{\star}\circ\bm{V}^{\star}+\frac{2}{\gamma^{2}}\bm{r}\circ\bm{V}^{\star}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\gamma}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})^{-1}\left(\gamma\bm{P}-\bm{I}\right)\left(\bm{V}^{\star}\circ\bm{V}^{\star}\right)+\frac{2}{\gamma^{2}}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})^{-1}\left(\bm{r}\circ\bm{V}^{\star}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\gamma^{2}}(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})^{-1}\left(\bm{r}\circ\bm{V}^{\star}\right)\leq\frac{2}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{2}}\bm{1},

where the second line makes use of the basic relation
\bm{V}^{\star}=\bm{r}+\gamma\bm{P}\bm{V}^{\star}
.
As a consequence, we conclude

\displaystyle(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})^{-1}\max_{k:\,t/2\leq k<t}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}\big(\bm{V}_{k}\big)

\displaystyle\leq(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})^{-1}\Big(\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}\big(\bm{V}^{\star}\big)+\frac{4}{1-\gamma}\max_{k:\,t/2\leq k<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{k}\|_{\infty}\bm{1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\gamma(1-\gamma)^{2}}\Big(1+2\max_{k:\,t/2\leq k<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{k}\|_{\infty}\Big)\bm{1}.

(122)

Here, the first inequality arises from (
97
), while the second inequality holds due to the facts that
\big\|(\bm{I}-\gamma\bm{P})^{-1}\big\|_{1}=1/(1-\gamma)
.

Step 4: putting everything together.

Consequently, substituting the bounds in Steps 2-3 into (
113
) yields

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}_{t}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 30\sqrt{\frac{\big(\log^{3}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{3}T}\bigg(1+\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\bigg)}\qquad\text{for all}\quad t\geq\frac{T}{c_{2}\log T}.

(123)

Repeating the same argument as in Section
B.4
, we see that

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}_{T}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq c_{9}\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{\big(\log^{3}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{3}T}}+\frac{\big(\log^{3}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{3}T}\bigg)

(124)

holds with probability at least
1-\delta
, where
c_{9}>0
is some universal constant.
As a result, one has

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}_{T}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\sqrt{\min\{\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{2}\}}+\min\{\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{2}\}\bigg)=\frac{1}{2}\left(\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\mathds{1}\{\varepsilon\leq 1\}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{2}\right)\mathds{1}\{\varepsilon>1\}\leq\varepsilon,

as long as the sample size satisfies the following

\frac{\big(\log^{3}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{3}T}\leq\frac{\min\{\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{2}\}}{c_{3}},

for some constant
c_{3}\geq\max\{1,2c_{9}\}
. This requirement is equivalent to condition (
103
) as claimed.

C.3
Proof for Remarks
2
and
3

Proof for Remark
2
.

Let us divide the dynamics of the algorithm into two parts.

•

For any
1\leq t\leq T/2
, it follows from Lemma
4
that

\displaystyle\bm{0}\leq\bm{V}_{T/2}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\bm{1}.

(125)

•

Next, let us consider any
T/2<t\leq T
, and set
\widetilde{t}=t-T/2
.
It comes from the choice (
13
) that

\frac{1}{1+\frac{\widetilde{c}_{1}(1-\gamma)(\widetilde{t}+T/2)}{\log^{2}(\widetilde{t}+T/2+1)}}\leq\eta_{\widetilde{t}+T/2}=\eta_{t}\leq\frac{1}{1+\frac{\widetilde{c}_{2}(1-\gamma)(\widetilde{t}+T/2)}{\log^{2}(\widetilde{t}+T/2+1)}}

\Longrightarrow\qquad\frac{1}{1+\frac{c_{1}(1-\gamma)(T/2)}{\log^{2}(T/2)}}\leq\eta_{\widetilde{t}+T/2}\leq\frac{1}{1+\frac{c_{2}(1-\gamma)\widetilde{t}}{\log^{2}(T/2)}},

provided that
\widetilde{c}_{1}
and
\widetilde{c}_{2}
are suitably chosen.
By treating
\bm{V}_{T/2}
as the initial point and invoking Theorem
1
,
we immediately establish (
9
) under the choice (
13
)
and the sample size condition (
14
).

Proof for Remark
3
.

Let us define, for each
1\leq t\leq T
,

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{t}\coloneqq\min\Bigg\{\sqrt{\frac{2c_{3}(\log^{3}T)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}|T}{\delta/T}\big)}{(1-\gamma)^{3}t}},\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\Bigg\}.

(126)

Invoking the claim in Remark
2
in conjunction with Lemma
4
reveals that with probability at least
1-\delta/T
,

\displaystyle\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\big|V_{t}(s)-V^{\star}(s)\big|\leq\varepsilon_{t}

for any given
t\leq T
.
Taking the union bound over all
1\leq t\leq T
implies that with probability exceeding
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\Big|\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}V_{t}(s)-V^{\star}(s)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\big|V_{t}(s)-V^{\star}(s)\big|\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{2c_{3}(\log^{3}T)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}|T^{2}}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)^{3}}}\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}\leq 4\sqrt{\frac{c_{3}(\log^{3}T)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)^{3}T}},

where the last inequality results from the elementary inequality
\sum_{t=1}^{T}1/\sqrt{t}\leq 2\sqrt{T}
. This finishes the proof.

Appendix D
Lower bound: sub-optimality of synchronous Q-learning (Theorem
3
)

In this section, a main focus is to establish the lower bound claimed in Theorem
3
by analyzing synchronous Q-learning for the MDP instance constructed in Section
4.3
. Without loss of generality, we assume

\log T\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}

(127)

throughout the proof; otherwise the lower bound in Theorem
3
is worse than the minimax lower bound
\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{3}T}
in
Azar et al., 2013
.

Throughout, we shall use
P_{t}
to represent the sample transitions such that for any triple
(s,a,s^{\prime})
,

\displaystyle P_{t}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)\coloneqq\begin{cases}1,\quad&\text{if }s_{t}(s,a)=s^{\prime},\\
0,&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(128)

where
s_{t}(s,a)
stands for the sample collected in the
t
-th iteration (see (
5
)).
Recognizing that state 2 is associated with a singleton action space, we shall often write

P_{t}(s^{\prime}\,|\,2)\coloneqq P_{t}(s^{\prime}\,|\,2,1)

for notational simplicity.

D.1
Key quantities related to learning rates

We find it convenient to define the following quantities (by abuse of notation)

\displaystyle\eta_{k}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\qquad\text{for any }1\leq k<t,

(129a)

\displaystyle\eta_{0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\prod_{i=1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big),

(129b)

\displaystyle\eta_{t}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\eta_{t}.

(129c)

It is helpful to establish several basic properties about these quantities.
As can be easily verified,

\displaystyle\eta_{0}^{(t)}+(1-\gamma p)\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}=\prod_{i=1}^{t}(1-\widehat{\eta}_{i})+\widehat{\eta}_{1}\prod_{i=2}^{t}(1-\widehat{\eta}_{i})+\widehat{\eta}_{2}\prod_{i=3}^{t}(1-\widehat{\eta}_{i})+\cdots+\widehat{\eta}_{t-1}(1-\widehat{\eta}_{t})+\widehat{\eta}_{t}=1,

(130)

where we denote
\widehat{\eta}_{i}\coloneqq\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)
to simplify notation.
Similarly, for any given integer
0\leq\tau<t
one has

\displaystyle\prod_{i=\tau+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)+(1-\gamma p)\sum_{k=\tau+1}^{t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}=1.

(131)

D.2
Preliminary calculations

Before moving forward, we record several basic relations as a result of the Q-learning update rule.

D.2.1
Basic update rules and expansion

Given that
Q_{0}=V_{0}=0
and that state 0 is absorbing, the update rule (
4
) gives

\displaystyle V_{t}(0)=Q_{t}(0,1)=\big(1-\eta_{t}(1-\gamma)\big)Q_{t-1}(0,1)=\prod_{i=1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big)Q_{0}(0,1)=0

(132)

for all
t\geq 1
.
Regarding state 2, the update rule (
4
) taken together with (
132
) leads to

\displaystyle V_{t}(2)=Q_{t}(2,1)

\displaystyle=\big(1-\eta_{t}\big)Q_{t-1}(2,1)+\eta_{t}\big\{r(2,1)+\gamma P_{t}(2\,|\,2)V_{t-1}(2)+\gamma P_{t}(0\,|\,2)V_{t-1}(0)\big\}

\displaystyle=\big(1-\eta_{t}\big)V_{t-1}(2)+\eta_{t}\big\{1+\gamma P_{t}(2\,|\,2)V_{t-1}(2)\big\},

(133)

and for state 3,

\displaystyle V_{t}(3)=Q_{t}(3,1)

\displaystyle=\big(1-\eta_{t}\big)Q_{t-1}(3,1)+\eta_{t}\big\{r(3,1)+\gamma V_{t-1}(3)\big\}

\displaystyle=\big(1-\eta_{t}(1-\gamma)\big)V_{t-1}(3)+\eta_{t}.

(134)

Similarly, one also has

\displaystyle Q_{t}(1,1)

\displaystyle=(1-\eta_{t})Q_{t-1}(1,1)+\eta_{t}\big\{1+\gamma P_{t}(1\,|\,1,1)V_{t-1}(1)\big\},

(135a)

\displaystyle Q_{t}(1,2)

\displaystyle=(1-\eta_{t})Q_{t-1}(1,2)+\eta_{t}\big\{1+\gamma P_{t}(1\,|\,1,2)V_{t-1}(1)\big\}.

(135b)

In what follows, we shall first determine a crude range for certain quantities relates to the learning rates
\eta_{t}
, and then combine this with the above relations to establish the desired result.

Next, we record some elementary decomposition of
V_{t}(2)
. For any iteration
t
and
\tau<t
, one can continue the derivation in (
133
) to obtain

\displaystyle V_{t}(2)

\displaystyle=\big(1-\eta_{t}(1-\gamma p)\big)V_{t-1}(2)+\eta_{t}\big\{1+\gamma\big(P_{t}(2\,|\,2)-p\big)V_{t-1}(2)\big\}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=\tau+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)V_{\tau}(2)+\sum_{k=\tau+1}^{t}\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\big\{1+\gamma\big(P_{k}(2\,|\,2)-p\big)V_{k-1}(2)\big\}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=\tau+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)V_{\tau}(2)+\sum_{k=\tau+1}^{t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}+\sum_{k=\tau+1}^{t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}\gamma\big(P_{k}(2\,|\,2)-p\big)V_{k-1}(2)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=\tau+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)V_{\tau}(2)+\frac{1-\prod_{i=\tau+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)}{1-\gamma p}+\sum_{k=\tau+1}^{t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}\gamma\big(P_{k}(2\,|\,2)-p\big)V_{k-1}(2)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma p}-\prod_{i=\tau+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\left[\frac{1}{1-\gamma p}-V_{\tau}(2)\right]+\sum_{k=\tau+1}^{t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}\gamma\big(P_{k}(2\,|\,2)-p\big)V_{k-1}(2),

(136)

where the penultimate line arises from (
131
).
In particular, in the special case where
\tau=0
(so that
V_{\tau}(2)=V_{0}(2)=0
), this simplifies to

\displaystyle V_{t}(2)=\frac{1-\eta_{0}^{(t)}}{1-\gamma p}+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}\gamma\big(P_{k}(2\,|\,2)-p\big)V_{k-1}(2),

(137)

which relies on the definition of
\eta_{0}^{(t)}
in (
129
).
With similar derivation, (
134
) leads to

\displaystyle V_{t}(3)=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\Big[1-\prod_{i=1}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big)\Big]=V^{\star}(3)-\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\prod_{i=1}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big).

(138)

D.2.2
Mean and variance of
V^{\star}(2)-V_{T}(2)

We start by computing the mean
V^{\star}(2)-\mathbb{E}[V_{t}(2)]
.
From the construction (
44
), it is easily seen that
\mathbb{E}[P_{k}(2\,|\,2)]=p
, which together with the identity (
137
) leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[V_{T}(2)\big]=\frac{1-\eta_{0}^{(T)}}{1-\gamma p}\qquad\text{and}\qquad V^{\star}(2)-\mathbb{E}\big[V_{T}(2)\big]=\frac{\eta_{0}^{(T)}}{1-\gamma p}.

(139)

Similarly, applying the above argument to (
136
) and rearranging terms, we immediately arrive at

\displaystyle V^{\star}(2)-\mathbb{E}\big[V_{T}(2)\big]=\prod_{i=\tau+1}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\left[\frac{1}{1-\gamma p}-\mathbb{E}\big[V_{\tau}(2)\big]\right]

(140)

for any integer
0\leq\tau<T
.

Next, we develop a lower bound on the variance
\mathsf{Var}\big(V_{T}(2)\big)
.
Towards this end, consider first a martingale sequence
\{Z_{k}\}_{0\leq k\leq T}
adapted to
a filtration
\mathcal{F}_{0}\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{1}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{T}
,
namely,
\mathbb{E}[Z_{k+1}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{k}]=0
and
\mathbb{E}[Z_{k}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{k}]=Z_{k}
for all
0\leq k\leq T
.
In addition, consider any
0\leq\tau<T
, and let
W_{0}
be a random variable such that
\mathbb{E}[W_{0}\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{\tau}]=W_{0}
.
Then the law of total variance together with basic martingale properties tells us that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}\left(W_{0}+\sum_{k=\tau+1}^{T}Z_{k}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{Var}\left(W_{0}+\sum_{k=\tau+1}^{T}Z_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{T-1}\right)\right]+\mathsf{Var}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[W_{0}+\sum_{k=\tau+1}^{T}Z_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{T-1}\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\,\mathsf{Var}\left(Z_{T}\mid\mathcal{F}_{T-1}\right)\big]+\mathsf{Var}\left(W_{0}+\sum_{k=\tau+1}^{T-1}Z_{k}\right)=\cdots

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=\tau+1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\big[\,\mathsf{Var}\left(Z_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right)\big]+\mathsf{Var}\left(W_{0}\right)\geq\sum_{k=\tau+1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\big[\,\mathsf{Var}\left(Z_{k}\mid\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right)\big].

(141)

Consequently, for any
0\leq\tau<T-1
, it follows from the decomposition (
136
) (with
\tau
replaced by
\tau+1
) that

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}\big(V_{T}(2)\big)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=\tau+2}^{T}\mathsf{Var}\Big(\eta_{k}^{(T)}\gamma\big(P_{k}(2\,|\,2)-p\big)V_{k-1}(2)\,|\,V_{k-1}(2)\Big)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=\tau+2}^{T}\big(\eta_{k}^{(T)}\gamma\big)^{2}p(1-p)\mathbb{E}\big[\big(V_{k-1}(2)\big)^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\left(1-\gamma\right)\left(4\gamma-1\right)}{9}\cdot\frac{1}{4(1-\gamma)^{2}}\sum_{k=\tau+2}^{T}\big(\eta_{k}^{(T)}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{4\gamma-1}{36\left(1-\gamma\right)}\sum_{k=\tau+2}^{T}\big(\eta_{k}^{(T)}\big)^{2},

(142)

where the first identity relies on the fact that
P_{k}(2\,|\,2)
is a Bernoulli random variable with mean
p
, and the inequality comes from the definition of
\tau
(see (
148
)) and the choice of
p
(see (
45
)). As an implication, the sum of squares of
\eta_{k}^{(T)}
plays a crucial role in determining the variance of
V_{T}(2)
.

D.3
Lower bounds for three cases

D.3.1
Case 1: small learning rates (
c_{\eta}\geq\log T
or
0\leq\eta\leq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)T}
)

In this case, we focus on lower bounding
V^{\star}(2)-\mathbb{E}\big[V_{T}(2)\big]
.
In view of this identity (
139
), this boils down to controlling
\eta_{0}^{(T)}
.

Suppose that
c_{\eta}>\log T
(for rescaled linear learning rates) or
0\leq\eta<\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)T}
(for constant learning rates).
A little algebra then gives

\displaystyle\eta_{t}(1-\gamma p)

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}\frac{1-\gamma p}{(1-\gamma)t\log T}=\frac{4}{3t\log T}\leq\frac{1}{2},\qquad&\text{if }\eta_{t}=\frac{1}{1+c_{\eta}(1-\gamma)t}\\
\frac{1-\gamma p}{(1-\gamma)T}=\frac{4}{3T}\leq\frac{1}{2},&\text{if }\eta_{t}=\eta\end{cases}

(143)

for any
t\geq 1
, provided that
T\geq 15
. Consequently, one can derive

\displaystyle\log\eta_{0}^{(T)}=\sum_{i=1}^{T}\log\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\geq-1.5\sum_{i=1}^{T}\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\geq-2,

(144)

where the first inequality holds due to the elementary fact
\log(1-x)\geq-1.5x
for all
0\leq x\leq 0.5
,
and the last inequality follows from the following bound (which makes use of (
143
))

\sum_{i=1}^{T}\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\leq\begin{cases}\frac{3}{4\log T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}\frac{1}{i}\leq 1,&\text{if }\eta_{t}=\frac{1}{1+c_{\eta}(1-\gamma)t}\\
\frac{4}{3T}\sum_{i=1}^{T}1=\frac{4}{3},&\text{if }\eta_{t}=\eta.\end{cases}

Combining the above result with the properties (
139
) and (
144
) then yields

V^{\star}(2)-\mathbb{E}\big[V_{T}(2)\big]=\frac{\eta_{0}^{(T)}}{1-\gamma p}\geq\frac{e^{-2}}{1-\gamma p}=\frac{3}{4e^{2}(1-\gamma)}.

(145)

This taken together with (
47
) gives

\mathbb{E}\big[\big(V^{\star}(2)-V_{T}(2)\big)^{2}\big]\geq\big(V^{\star}(2)-\mathbb{E}\big[V_{T}(2)\big]\big)^{2}\geq\frac{9}{16e^{4}(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

(146)

D.3.2
Case 2: large learning rates (
c_{\eta}\leq 1-\gamma
or
\eta\geq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}T}
)

By virtue of (
140
), the mean gap
V^{\star}(2)-\mathbb{E}\big[V_{T}(2)\big]
depends on two factors: (i) the choice of the learning rates, and (ii) the gap between
\frac{1}{1-\gamma p}
and
\mathbb{E}\big[V_{\tau}(2)\big]
, where
\tau
is an integer obeying
0\leq\tau<T
.
To control the factor (ii), we need to choose
\tau
properly.
Let us start by considering the simple scenario with
\mathbb{E}\big[\big(V_{T}(2)\big)^{2}\big]<\frac{1}{4(1-\gamma)^{2}}
, for which we have

\displaystyle V^{\star}(2)-\mathbb{E}\big[V_{T}(2)\big]\geq\frac{3}{4(1-\gamma)}-\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[\big(V_{T}(2)\big)^{2}\big]}\geq\frac{1}{4(1-\gamma)}.

(147)

Here, we have used (
46
) and the elementary fact
\mathbb{E}[X]\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]}
. Consequently, it remains to look at the scenario obeying
\mathbb{E}\big[\big(V_{T}(2)\big)^{2}\big]\geq\frac{1}{4(1-\gamma)^{2}}
, towards which we propose to set
\tau
as follows

\displaystyle\tau\coloneqq\min\left\{0\leq\tau^{\prime}\leq T-1\,\,\Big|\,\,\mathbb{E}\big[\big(V_{t}(2)\big)^{2}\big]\geq\frac{1}{4(1-\gamma)^{2}}\penalty\ \text{for all }\tau^{\prime}+1\leq t\leq T\right\}.

(148)

Clearly,
\tau
is well-defined in this scenario and obeys (in view of both (
148
) and the initialization
V_{0}=0
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big(V_{\tau}(2)\big)^{2}\big]<\frac{1}{4(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

(149)

Our analysis for this scenario is divided into three subcases based on the size of the learning rates.

Case 2.1.

Consider the case where

\displaystyle\prod_{i=\tau+1}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\geq\frac{1}{2}.

(150)

Invoke (
140
) to deduce that

\displaystyle V^{\star}(2)-\mathbb{E}\big[V_{T}(2)\big]

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=\tau+1}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\Big[\frac{1}{1-\gamma p}-\mathbb{E}\big[V_{\tau}(2)\big]\Big]

\displaystyle\geq\prod_{i=\tau+1}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\Big[\frac{3}{4(1-\gamma)}-\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[\big(V_{\tau}(2)\big)^{2}\big]}\Big]

\displaystyle\geq\prod_{i=\tau+1}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\frac{1}{4(1-\gamma)}\geq\frac{1}{8(1-\gamma)},

where the second line makes use of the definition (
45
) and the elementary fact
\mathbb{E}[X]\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[X^{2}]}
,
and the last line relies on the inequalities (
149
) and (
150
).

Case 2.2.

We now move on to the case where

\displaystyle 0\leq\prod_{i=\tau+1}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\leq\frac{1}{2}.

(151)

We intend to demonstrate that the variance of
V_{T}(2)
—and hence the typical size of its fluctuation—is too large.
In view of the observation (
142
), it boils down to lower bounding
\sum_{k=\tau+2}^{T}\big(\eta_{k}^{(T)}\big)^{2}
, which we accomplish as follows.

•

Consider constant learning rates
\eta_{k}=\eta
, and suppose that
\eta
obeys
\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}T}<\eta\leq 1<\frac{1}{1-\gamma p}
.
It is readily seen that
\eta_{k}^{(T)}=\eta\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma p)\big)^{T-k}
for any
k\geq 1
.
We claim that it suffices to focus on the scenario where

\displaystyle\tau\leq T-2.

(152)

In fact, if
\tau\geq T-1
, then the definition (
148
) of
\tau
necessarily requires that

\mathbb{E}\big[V_{T-1}(2)\big]\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[\big(V_{T-1}(2)\big)^{2}\big]}<\frac{1}{2(1-\gamma)}.

In view of (
139
) (with
T
replaced by
T-1
), a little algebra shows that this is equivalent to
\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma p)\big)^{T-1}\geq 1/3
,
and hence
\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma p)\big)^{T}\geq 1/9
.
In turn, this combined with (
139
) leads to

\displaystyle V^{\star}(2)-\mathbb{E}\big[V_{T}(2)\big]=\frac{\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma p)\big)^{T}}{1-\gamma p}=\frac{3\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma p)\big)^{T}}{4(1-\gamma)}\geq\frac{1}{12(1-\gamma)},

(153)

which already suffices for our purpose.

Next, assuming that (
152
) holds, one can derive

\displaystyle\sum_{k=\tau+2}^{T}\big(\eta_{k}^{(T)}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=\tau+2}^{T}\eta^{2}\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma p)\big)^{2(T-k)}=\frac{\eta^{2}\big[1-\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma p)\big)^{2(T-\tau-1)}\big]}{1-\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma p)\big)^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\eta^{2}/2}{1-\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma p)\big)^{2}}\geq\frac{3\eta}{16(1-\gamma)},

(154)

where the first inequality holds since (from the assumptions (
151
) and
\tau\leq T-2
)

0\leq\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma p)\big)^{2(T-\tau-1)}\leq\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma p)\big)^{T-\tau}=\prod_{i=\tau+1}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\leq\frac{1}{2},

and the last inequality follows since

0\leq 1-\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma p)\big)^{2}=1-\Big(1-\frac{4\eta(1-\gamma)}{3}\Big)^{2}\leq\frac{8\eta(1-\gamma)}{3}.

Substituting (
154
) into (
142
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}\big(V_{T}(2)\big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{4\gamma-1}{36(1-\gamma)}\sum_{k=\tau+1}^{T}\big(\eta_{k}^{(T)}\big)^{2}\geq\frac{2}{36(1-\gamma)}\cdot\frac{3\eta}{16(1-\gamma)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta}{96(1-\gamma)^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{96(1-\gamma)^{4}T},

(155)

provided that
\gamma\geq 3/4
(so that
4\gamma-1\geq 2
).
Here, the last inequality is valid since either
\eta\geq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}T}
.

•

We then move on to linearly rescaled learning rates with
\eta_{t}=\frac{1}{1+c_{\eta}(1-\gamma)t}
for some
0\leq c_{\eta}<1-\gamma
.
Towards this, we first make the observation that

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{k-1}^{(T)}}{\eta_{k}^{(T)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta_{k-1}\big(1-\eta_{k}(1-\gamma p)\big)}{\eta_{k}}=\frac{1-\frac{4}{3}(1-\gamma)\eta_{k}}{1-\big(\frac{1}{\eta_{k}}-\frac{1}{\eta_{k-1}}\big)\eta_{k}}=\frac{1-\frac{4}{3}(1-\gamma)\eta_{k}}{1-c_{\eta}(1-\gamma)\eta_{k}}=1-\frac{\big(\frac{4}{3}-c_{\eta}\big)(1-\gamma)\eta_{k}}{1-c_{\eta}(1-\gamma)\eta_{k}}

\displaystyle\leq 1-(1-\gamma)\eta_{k}\leq 1-(1-\gamma)\eta_{T},

(156)

with the proviso that
c_{\eta}<1-\gamma\leq 1/3
(as long as
\gamma\geq 2/3
).
By defining
\tau^{\prime}\coloneqq T-\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)\eta_{T}}
, one can deduce that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=\tau+2}^{T}\big(\eta_{k}^{(T)}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{k=\max\{\tau+2,\tau^{\prime}+1\}}^{T}\big(\eta_{k}^{(T)}\big)^{2}\geq\frac{1}{T-\max\{\tau+1,\tau^{\prime}\}}\left[\sum_{k=\max\{\tau+2,\tau^{\prime}+1\}}^{T}\eta_{k}^{(T)}\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\geq(1-\gamma)\eta_{T}\left[\sum_{k=\max\{\tau+2,\tau^{\prime}+1\}}^{T}\eta_{k}^{(T)}\right]^{2},

(157)

where the penultimate inequality comes from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
In addition, recognizing that
\eta_{k_{1}}^{(T)}\leq\big(1-(1-\gamma)\eta_{T}\big)^{k_{2}-k_{1}}\eta_{k_{2}}^{(T)}
for any
k_{2}\geq k_{1}
(see (
156
)), one has

\displaystyle\sum_{k=\tau^{\prime}+1}^{T}\eta_{k}^{(T)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=\tau^{\prime}+1}^{T}\eta_{k}^{(T)},

\displaystyle\sum_{k=\max\{2\tau^{\prime}-T+1,1\}}^{\tau^{\prime}}\eta_{k}^{(T)}

\displaystyle\leq\big(1-(1-\gamma)\eta_{T}\big)^{T-\tau^{\prime}}\sum_{k=\tau^{\prime}+1}^{T}\eta_{k}^{(T)},

\displaystyle\sum_{k=\max\{3\tau^{\prime}-2T+1,1\}}^{2\tau^{\prime}-T}\eta_{k}^{(T)}

\displaystyle\leq\big(1-(1-\gamma)\eta_{T}\big)^{2(T-\tau^{\prime})}\sum_{k=\tau^{\prime}+1}^{T}\eta_{k}^{(T)},

\displaystyle\cdots

Summing these inequalities up and rearranging terms, we reach

\displaystyle\sum_{k=\tau^{\prime}+1}^{T}\eta_{k}^{(T)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{T}\eta_{k}^{(T)}}{1+\big(1-(1-\gamma)\eta_{T}\big)^{T-\tau^{\prime}}+\big(1-(1-\gamma)\eta_{T}\big)^{2(T-\tau^{\prime})}+\ldots}\geq\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{T}\eta_{k}^{(T)}}{\frac{1}{1-\big(1-(1-\gamma)\eta_{T}\big)^{T-\tau^{\prime}}}}

\displaystyle=\left(1-\big(1-(1-\gamma)\eta_{T}\big)^{T-\tau^{\prime}}\right)\sum_{k=1}^{T}\eta_{k}^{(T)}\geq(1-e^{-1})\sum_{k=1}^{T}\eta_{k}^{(T)},

which relies on the fact
\big(1-(1-\gamma)\eta_{T}\big)^{T-\tau^{\prime}}=\big(1-1/(T-\tau^{\prime})\big)^{T-\tau^{\prime}}\leq e^{-1}
(using the definition of
\tau^{\prime}
).
Consequently, it is easily seen that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=\max\{\tau+2,\tau^{\prime}+1\}}^{T}\eta_{k}^{(T)}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\sum_{k=\tau+2}^{T}\eta_{k}^{(T)},\sum_{k=\tau^{\prime}+1}^{T}\eta_{k}^{(T)}\right\}\geq(1-e^{-1})\sum_{k=\tau+2}^{T}\eta_{k}^{(T)}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{=}(1-e^{-1})\left[1-\prod_{i=\tau+2}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\right]\frac{1}{1-\gamma p}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(ii)}}{\geq}\left[1-\frac{1}{2\big(1-\eta_{\tau+1}(1-\gamma p)\big)}\right]\frac{1-e^{-1}}{1-\gamma p}\overset{\text{(iii)}}{\geq}\frac{1-e^{-1}}{4(1-\gamma p)}\geq\frac{3}{32(1-\gamma)}.

Here, (i) and (ii) follow from (
131
) and (
151
), respectively, while (iii) holds since

\eta_{\tau+1}(1-\gamma p)\leq 1-\gamma p=\frac{4(1-\gamma)}{3}\leq\frac{1}{3}

as long as
\gamma\geq 3/4
.
Substitution into (
157
) yields

\displaystyle\sum_{k=\tau+2}^{T}\big(\eta_{k}^{(T)}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{9\eta_{T}}{1024(1-\gamma)}.

(158)

Substituting the above bound into (
142
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}\big(V_{T}(2)\big)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{4\gamma-1}{36(1-\gamma)}\sum_{k=\tau+1}^{T}\big(\eta_{k}^{(T)}\big)^{2}\geq\frac{2}{36(1-\gamma)}\cdot\frac{9\eta_{T}}{1024(1-\gamma)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta_{T}}{2048(1-\gamma)^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{4096(1-\gamma)^{4}T},

(159)

provided that
\gamma\geq 3/4
(so that
4\gamma-1\geq 2
).
Here, the last inequality is valid since
\eta_{T}=\frac{1}{1+c_{\eta}(1-\gamma)T}\geq\frac{1}{1+(1-\gamma)^{2}T}\geq\frac{1}{2(1-\gamma)^{2}T}
as long as
T\geq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}
.

Putting all this together.

With the above bounds in place, it is readily seen that either the bias is too large (see (
153
))
or the variance is too large (see (
155
) and (
159
)). These bounds taken collectively with (
47
) yield

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big(V^{\star}(2)-V_{T}(2)\big)^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\geq\big(V^{\star}(2)-\mathbb{E}\big[V_{T}(2)\big]\big)^{2}+\mathsf{Var}\big(V_{T}(2)\big)

\displaystyle\geq\min\left\{\frac{1}{144(1-\gamma)^{2}},\frac{1}{96(1-\gamma)^{4}T},\frac{1}{4096(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\right\}=\frac{1}{4096(1-\gamma)^{4}T},

(160)

provided
T\geq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}
.

D.3.3
Case 3: medium learning rates (
1-\gamma<c_{\eta}<\log T
or
\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)T}\leq\eta\leq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}T}
)

Throughout this case, we assume that

\eta_{0}^{(T)}\leq\frac{1}{75}.

(161)

In fact, if
\eta_{0}^{(T)}>1/75
, then the scenario becomes much easier to cope with.
To see this, applying the previous result (
145
) and recalling the choice (
45
) of
p
immediately yield

\displaystyle V^{\star}(2)-\mathbb{E}\big[V_{T}(2)\big]\geq\frac{\eta_{0}^{(T)}}{1-\gamma p}>\frac{1}{100(1-\gamma)},

(162)

which together with (
47
) and the assumption
T\geq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big(V^{\star}(2)-V_{T}(2)\big)^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\geq\big(V^{\star}(2)-\mathbb{E}\big[V_{T}(2)\big]\big)^{2}\geq\frac{1}{10000(1-\gamma)^{2}}\geq\frac{1}{10000(1-\gamma)^{4}T}.

(163)

We now turn our attention to the dynamics w.r.t. state
1

and its associated value function
V_{t}(1)
under the condition (
161
).

Two auxiliary sequences.

Towards this, we first eliminate the effect of initialization on
Q_{t}(1,a)
by introducing the following auxiliary sequence

\displaystyle\widehat{Q}_{t}(a)=(1-\eta_{t})\widehat{Q}_{t-1}(a)+\eta_{t}\big\{1+\gamma P_{t}(1\,|\,1,a)\widehat{V}_{t-1}\big\},

(164)

with

\displaystyle\widehat{V}_{t-1}\coloneqq\max_{a}\widehat{Q}_{t-1}(a)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\widehat{Q}_{0}(a)\coloneqq Q^{\star}(1,a)=\frac{1}{1-\gamma p},

where we recall the value of
Q^{\star}(1,a)
from Lemma
3
.
In other words,
\{\widehat{Q}_{t}(a)\}
is essentially a Q-learning sequence when initialized at the ground truth.
Despite the difference in initialization,
we claim that the discrepancy between
\widehat{Q}_{t}(a)
and
Q_{t}(1,a)
can be well controlled in the following sense:

\displaystyle Q_{t}(1,a)\geq\widehat{Q}_{t}(a)-\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\prod_{i=1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big),\qquad a\in\{1,2\},

(165)

which shall be justified in Section
D.3.4
. As we shall discuss momentarily, the gap
\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\prod_{i=1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big)
is sufficiently small for this case.

Further, in order to control
\widehat{Q}_{t}(a)
, we find it convenient to introduce another auxiliary sequence as follows

\displaystyle\overline{Q}_{t}=(1-\eta_{t})\overline{Q}_{t-1}+\eta_{t}\big\{1+\gamma P_{t}(1\,|\,1,1)\overline{Q}_{t-1}\big\}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\overline{Q}_{0}=V^{\star}(1)=\frac{1}{1-\gamma p},

(166)

which can be interpreted as a Q-learning sequence when there is only a single action (so that there is no max operator involved).
In view of the basic fact that
\widehat{V}_{t}=\max_{a}\widehat{Q}_{t}(a)\geq\widehat{Q}_{t}(1)
, we can easily verify that

\displaystyle\widehat{Q}_{t}(1)\geq(1-\eta_{t})\widehat{Q}_{t-1}(1)+\eta_{t}\big\{1+\gamma P_{t}(1\,|\,1,1)\widehat{Q}_{t-1}(1)\big\}\geq\overline{Q}_{t},

(167)

allowing one to lower bound
\widehat{V}_{t}
by controlling
\overline{Q}_{t}
.

A useful lower bound on the auxiliary sequence (
164
).

In what follows, let us establish a useful lower bound on the sequence (
164
) introduced above.
Then we claim that there exists some
\tau\leq T
(see (
181
) and (
183
)) such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big\{\widehat{V}_{t}\geq\frac{1}{4(1-\gamma)}\Big\}\geq\frac{1}{2},\qquad\text{for}\penalty\ t\geq\tau.

(168)

The auxiliary sequence constructed in (
166
) plays a crucial role in establishing this claim.

Proof of the claim (
168
).

We intend to employ the sequence
\overline{Q}_{t}
(cf. (
166
)) to help control
\widehat{V}_{t}
.
It is first observed that the sequence
\overline{Q}_{t}
admits the following decomposition (akin to the derivation in (
137
))

\displaystyle\overline{Q}_{t}

\displaystyle=\big(1-\eta_{t}(1-\gamma p)\big)\overline{Q}_{t-1}+\eta_{t}\big\{1+\gamma\big(P_{t}(1\,|\,1,1)-p\big)\overline{Q}_{t-1}\big\}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\overline{Q}_{0}+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\big\{1+\gamma\big(P_{k}(1\,|\,1,1)-p\big)\overline{Q}_{k-1}\big\}

\displaystyle=\eta_{0}^{(t)}\frac{1}{1-\gamma p}+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}\gamma\big(P_{k}(1\,|\,1,1)-p\big)\overline{Q}_{k-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\gamma p}+\sum_{k=1}^{t}\underset{\eqqcolon\,z_{k}}{\underbrace{\eta_{k}^{(t)}\gamma\big(P_{k}(1\,|\,1,1)-p\big)\overline{Q}_{k-1}}},

(169)

where the last line results from (
130
). In order to lower bound
\overline{Q}_{t}
, it boils down to controlling
\sum_{k}z_{k}
.

Note that the sequence
\{z_{k}\}
defined above is a martingale satisfying

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[z_{k}\,|\,

\displaystyle P_{k-1}(1\,|\,1,1),\ldots,P_{1}(1\,|\,1,1)\big]=0

and

\displaystyle\qquad|z_{k}|\leq\max_{1\leq k\leq t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}\cdot\frac{\gamma p}{1-\gamma},

where the last inequality follows from the basic property
0\leq\overline{Q}_{k-1}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
(akin to Lemma
4
) and the fact that
\big|P_{k}(1\,|\,1,1)-p\big|\leq\max\{p,1-p\}=p
since
p=(4\gamma-1)/(3\gamma)
and
\gamma\geq 3/4
. We intend to invoke Freedman’s inequality to control (
169
).
Armed with these properties and the fact that
P_{k}(1\,|\,1,1)
is a Bernoulli random variable with mean
p
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{t}\mathsf{Var}\Big(z_{k}\,|\,P_{k-1}(1\,|\,1,1),\ldots,P_{1}(1\,|\,1,1)\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{t}\big(\eta_{k}^{(t)}\big)^{2}p(1-p)\big(\gamma\overline{Q}_{k-1}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq k\leq t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}\cdot\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}\cdot\frac{1}{3(1-\gamma)}\leq\frac{\max_{1\leq k\leq t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}}{4(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

Here, the penultimate inequality relies on the fact
0\leq\overline{Q}_{k-1}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
(akin to Lemma
4
) and the choice of
p
(see definition (
45
)),
whereas the last inequality results from the following condition (derived through (
130
))

\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}=\big(1-\eta_{0}^{(t)}\big)\frac{1}{1-\gamma p}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma p}=\frac{3}{4(1-\gamma)}.

Applying Freedman’s inequality (see (
60
)) then yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{t}z_{k}\bigg|\geq\sqrt{\frac{4\max_{1\leq k\leq t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\log\frac{2}{\delta}}+\frac{4\max_{1\leq k\leq t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}}{3(1-\gamma)}\log\frac{2}{\delta}\right\}\leq\delta.

(170)

As an implication of the preceding result, a key ingredient towards bounding
\sum_{k=1}^{t}z_{k}
lies in controlling the quantity
\max_{1\leq k\leq t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}
. To do so, we claim for the moment that there exists some
\tau\leq T
such that

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k\leq t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}\leq\frac{1}{50},\qquad\text{for}\penalty\ t\geq\tau,

(171)

whose proof is postponed to Section
D.3.4
.
In light of this claim, setting
\delta=1/2
in the expression (
170
) yields

\sum_{k=1}^{t}z_{k}\geq-\frac{1}{2(1-\gamma)}

with probably at least
1/2
. Combining this with the decomposition (
169
) and the property (
167
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\widehat{V}_{t}\geq\widehat{Q}_{t}(1)\geq\overline{Q}_{t}\geq\frac{1}{1-\gamma p}-\frac{1}{2(1-\gamma)}=\frac{1}{4(1-\gamma)}

with probability at least
1/2
, where the last identity relies on the choice of
p
(see the definition (
45
)).
This establishes the advertised claim (
168
).
∎

Main proof.

With the property (
168
) in place, we are positioned to prove our main result.
Towards this, we find it convenient to define

\displaystyle\Delta_{t}(a)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\widehat{Q}_{t}(a)-Q^{\star}(1,a),\qquad a=1,2;

(172a)

\displaystyle\Delta_{t,\mathsf{max}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\max_{a}\Delta_{t}(a).

(172b)

The goal is thus to control
\Delta_{T,\mathsf{max}}
; in fact, we intend to show that
\Delta_{T,\mathsf{max}}
is in expectation excessively large, resulting in an “over-estimation” issue that hinders convergence.
Towards this, it follows from the iterative update rule (
164
) that

\displaystyle\Delta_{t}(a)

\displaystyle=(1-\eta_{t})\Delta_{t-1}(a)+\eta_{t}\big(1+\gamma P_{t}(1\,|\,1,a)\widehat{V}_{t-1}-Q^{\star}(1,a)\big)

\displaystyle=(1-\eta_{t})\Delta_{t-1}(a)+\eta_{t}\gamma\big(P_{t}(1\,|\,1,a)\widehat{V}_{t-1}-pV^{\star}(1)\big)

\displaystyle=(1-\eta_{t})\Delta_{t-1}(a)+\eta_{t}\gamma\big(p\big(\widehat{V}_{t-1}-V^{\star}(1)\big)+\big(P_{t}(1\,|\,1,a)-p\big)\widehat{V}_{t-1}\big)

\displaystyle=(1-\eta_{t})\Delta_{t-1}(a)+\eta_{t}\gamma\Big(p\Delta_{t-1,\mathsf{max}}+\big(P_{t}(1\,|\,1,a)-p\big)\widehat{V}_{t-1}\Big).

Here, the second line comes from the Bellman equation
Q^{\star}(1,a)=1+\gamma pV^{\star}(1)
,
whereas the last line holds since
\widehat{V}_{t-1}-V^{\star}(1)=\max_{a}\big(\widehat{Q}_{t-1}(a)-V^{\star}(1)\big)=\max_{a}\Delta_{t-1}(a)
(as a consequence of the relation (
46
)).
Applying the above relation recursively leads to

\displaystyle\Delta_{t}(a)=\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\gamma\Big(p\Delta_{k-1,\mathsf{max}}+\big(P_{k}(1\,|\,1,a)-p\big)\widehat{V}_{k-1}\Big),

(173)

where we have used the initialization
\Delta_{0}(a)=0
. Letting

\displaystyle\xi_{t}(a)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\gamma\big(P_{k}(1\,|\,1,a)-p\big)\widehat{V}_{k-1},

(174a)

\displaystyle\xi_{t,\mathsf{max}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\max_{a}\xi_{t}(a),

(174b)

one can express the above relation as follows

\displaystyle\Delta_{t,\mathsf{max}}=\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\gamma p\Delta_{k-1,\mathsf{max}}+\xi_{t,\mathsf{max}}.

Next, we claim that
\mathbb{E}[\xi_{t,\mathsf{max}}]
satisfies the following property

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\xi_{t,\mathsf{max}}]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c}{\sqrt{(1-\gamma)^{2}T}\log T}\qquad\text{for all}\penalty\ t\geq\widehat{\tau}

(175)

for some universal constant
c>0
, where

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}\coloneqq\max\Bigg\{\tau^{\prime}\,\,\Big|\,\,\prod_{i=\tau^{\prime}}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\leq\frac{6}{7}\Bigg\},

(176)

whose existence is ensured under the condition (
161
).
Given the validity of this claim (which we shall justify in Section
D.3.4
), we immediately arrive at

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{t,\mathsf{max}}]

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\gamma p\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{k-1,\mathsf{max}}]+\frac{c}{\sqrt{(1-\gamma)^{2}T}\log T}\qquad\text{for all}\penalty\ t\geq\widehat{\tau}.

(177)

In order to study the above recursion, it is helpful to look at the following sequence
