\displaystyle x_{t}=(1-\eta_{t})x_{t-1}+\eta_{t}\bigg(\gamma px_{t-1}+\frac{c}{\sqrt{(1-\gamma)^{2}T}\log T}\bigg)

(178)

with
x_{\widehat{\tau}}=0
, where we recall the definition of
\widehat{\tau}
in (
176
).
In comparison to the iterative relation (
177
) which starts from
\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{0,\mathsf{max}}]=0
(and hence
\mathbb{E}[\Delta_{t,\mathsf{max}}]\geq 0
), we let the sequence
x_{t}
start from
x_{\widehat{\tau}}=0
, where
\widehat{\tau}
is defined in (
176
).
It is straightforward to verify that

\mathbb{E}\big[\Delta_{T,\mathsf{max}}\big]\geq x_{T},

(179)

recognizing that

\displaystyle x_{t}=\sum_{k=\widehat{\tau}}^{t}\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\gamma px_{k-1}+\sum_{k=\widehat{\tau}}^{T}\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i})\frac{c}{\sqrt{(1-\gamma)^{2}T}\log T}.

A little algebra reveals that the sequence (
178
) obeys

\displaystyle x_{T}

\displaystyle=\frac{c}{\sqrt{(1-\gamma)^{2}T}\log T}\sum_{k=\widehat{\tau}}^{T}\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)=\frac{c}{\sqrt{(1-\gamma)^{2}T}\log T}\frac{1}{1-\gamma p}\left[1-\prod_{i=\widehat{\tau}}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{3c}{4\sqrt{(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\log T}\left[1-\prod_{i=\widehat{\tau}}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\right]\geq\frac{3c}{28\sqrt{(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\log T},

where the second equality arises from (
131
),
and the last inequality holds as long as
\prod_{i=\widehat{\tau}}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\leq 6/7
(see (
191
)).
This taken together with (
179
) leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\Delta_{T,\mathsf{max}}\big]\geq x_{T}\geq\frac{3c}{28\sqrt{(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\log T}.

Combining the above bound with (
165
) leads to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[V_{T}(1)-V^{\star}(1)\Big]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\Big[\Delta_{T,\mathsf{max}}-\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\prod_{i=1}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{3c}{28\sqrt{(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\log T}-\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\prod_{i=1}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big).

Taking this together with (
138
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\max\Big\{

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|V_{T}(3)-V^{\star}(3)\big|\Big],\;\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|V_{T}(1)-V^{\star}(1)\big|\Big]\Big\}

\displaystyle\geq\max\left\{\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\prod_{i=1}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big),\penalty\ \frac{3c}{28\sqrt{(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\log T}-\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\prod_{i=1}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big)\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\prod_{i=1}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big)+\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{3c}{28\sqrt{(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\log T}-\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\prod_{i=1}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{3c}{56\sqrt{(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\log T}.

This combined with (
47
) establishes the following desired lower bound:

\displaystyle\max_{s}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|V_{T}(s)-V^{\star}(s)\big|^{2}\Big]\geq\left(\frac{3c}{56\sqrt{(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\log T}\right)^{2}=\frac{9c^{2}}{56^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T\log^{2}T}.

D.3.4
Proofs of auxiliary results

Proof of the inequality (
165
).

We shall establish this claim by induction.
To begin with, the inequality (
165
) holds trivially for the base case with
t=0
.
Now, let us assume that the claim holds up to the
(t-1)
-th iteration, and we would like to justify it for the
t
-th iteration.
As an immediate consequence of the claim (
165
) for the
(t-1)
-th iteration and the definitions of
V_{t-1}
and
\widehat{V}_{t-1}
, we have

\displaystyle V_{t-1}(1)=\max_{a}Q_{t-1}(1,a)

\displaystyle\geq\max_{a}\widehat{Q}_{t-1}(a)-\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\prod_{i=1}^{t-1}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big)

\displaystyle=\widehat{V}_{t-1}-\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\prod_{i=1}^{t-1}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big).

By virtue of the respective update rules of
Q_{t}(1,a)
and
\widehat{Q}_{t}(a)
, we can express their difference as follows:

\displaystyle Q_{t}(1,a)-\widehat{Q}_{t}(a)

\displaystyle=(1-\eta_{t})\big(Q_{t-1}(1,a)-\widehat{Q}_{t-1}(a)\big)+\eta_{t}\gamma P_{t}(1\,|\,1,a)\big(V_{t-1}(1)-\widehat{V}_{t-1}\big)

\displaystyle\geq-(1-\eta_{t})\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\prod_{i=1}^{t-1}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big)-\eta_{t}\gamma P_{t}(1\,|\,1,a)\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\prod_{i=1}^{t-1}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big)

\displaystyle\geq-(1-\eta_{t})\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\prod_{i=1}^{t-1}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big)-\eta_{t}\gamma\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\prod_{i=1}^{t-1}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\prod_{i=1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma)\big),

where the first inequality invokes the induction hypothesis for the
(t-1)
-th iteration.
This establishes (
165
) for the
t
-th iteration, and hence the proof is complete via an induction argument.

Proof of the claim (
171
).

When taking the constant learning rates
\eta_{t}\equiv\eta\leq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}T}\leq\frac{1}{50}
(under the condition
T\geq\frac{50}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}
), one has

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k\leq t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}\leq\eta_{t}=\eta\leq\frac{1}{50},

thus allowing us to take
\tau=1
for this case.

It then suffices to look at rescaled linear learning rates (i.e.,
\eta_{t}=\frac{1}{1+c_{\eta}(1-\gamma)t}
).
As already calculated in the expression (
156
), the ratio of two consecutive quantities obeys

\displaystyle\frac{\eta_{k-1}^{(t)}}{\eta_{k}^{(t)}}=\frac{1-\frac{4}{3}(1-\gamma)\eta_{k}}{1-c_{\eta}(1-\gamma)\eta_{k}}.

(180)

In what follows, we divide into two cases, depending on whether this sequence is decreasing or increasing.

•

The case with
4/3\leq c_{\eta}<\log T
.

In this scenario, the ratio in (
180
) is larger than 1, and hence the sequence
\{\eta_{k}^{(t)}\}
decreases with
k
.
Let us define

\displaystyle\tau\coloneqq\min\Bigg\{\tau^{\prime}\,\,\Big|\,\,\prod_{i=1}^{\tau^{\prime}}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\leq\frac{1}{50}\Bigg\},

(181)

which clearly satisfies
\tau\leq T
(in view of (
161
)).
For all
t\geq\tau
, one has

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k\leq t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}\leq\prod_{i=1}^{\tau}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\leq\frac{1}{50}.

At the same time, we claim that one must have

\displaystyle\prod_{i=\tau}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\leq\frac{2}{3}.

(182)

Otherwise, recalling
\eta_{0}^{(T)}=\prod_{i=1}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)
, we have

\eta_{0}^{(T)}=\left\{\prod_{i=1}^{\tau-1}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\right\}\left\{\prod_{i=\tau}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\right\}>\frac{1}{50}\cdot\frac{2}{3}=\frac{1}{75},

which contradicts our assumption that
\eta_{0}^{(T)}>1/75
(cf. (
161
)).

•

The case with
1-\gamma<c_{\eta}<4/3
.

In this case, the sequence
\eta_{k}^{(t)}
increases with
k
.
If we set

\displaystyle\tau\coloneqq\Big\lceil\frac{49}{c_{\eta}(1-\gamma)}\Big\rceil<\frac{50}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}<T,

(183)

then for all
t\geq\tau
we have

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k\leq t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}=\eta_{t}^{(t)}=\eta_{t}\leq\eta_{\tau}\leq\frac{1}{1+c_{\eta}(1-\gamma)\frac{49}{c_{\eta}(1-\gamma)}}=\frac{1}{50}.

Under the condition
T\geq\frac{150}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\geq\frac{150}{c_{\eta}(1-\gamma)}
(so that
T-\tau+1\geq\frac{100}{c_{\eta}(1-\gamma)}\geq\frac{100}{(1-\gamma)4/3}
),
one can show that

\displaystyle\prod_{i=\tau}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\leq\Big(1-\frac{1-\gamma}{100}\Big)^{T-\tau+1}\leq\Big(1-\frac{1-\gamma}{100}\Big)^{\frac{100}{(1-\gamma)4/3}}\leq\frac{3}{4}.

(184)

•

Putting these two cases together (with
\tau
specified in (
181
) and (
183
)), we obtain

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq k\leq t}\eta_{k}^{(t)}\leq\frac{1}{50}

(185)

for all
t\geq\tau
, thus establishing the desired inequality (
171
).

Proof of the inequality (
175
).

For every
t
, recalling the definition (
174
), it is convenient to write

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\xi_{t,\mathsf{max}}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\xi_{t}(1)+\xi_{t}(2)+|\xi_{t}(1)-\xi_{t}(2)|}{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{|\xi_{t}(1)-\xi_{t}(2)|}{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\,\left|\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\gamma\big(P_{k}(1\,|\,1,1)-P_{k}(1\,|\,1,2)\big)\widehat{V}_{k-1})\,\right|\right],

where we have used the fact that
\mathbb{E}[\xi_{t}(a)]=0
.
To control the right-hand side of the above equation, let us define

\displaystyle\zeta_{t}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{k=1}^{t}z_{k},\qquad z_{k}\coloneqq\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\gamma\big(P_{k}(1\,|\,1,1)-P_{k}(1\,|\,1,2)\big)\widehat{V}_{k-1}

for any
k\geq 1
,
where
\{z_{k}\}
also forms a martingale sequence since

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[z_{k}\,|\,\big\{P_{j}(1\,|\,1,1),P_{j}(1\,|\,1,2)\big\}_{1\leq j<k}\Big]=0.

As a consequence of Freedman’s inequality, we claim that
\zeta_{t}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\big|\zeta_{t}\big|\geq\sqrt{\frac{8\log\frac{2}{\delta}}{3(1-\gamma)}\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}^{2}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]^{2}}+\frac{4\eta_{t}\log\frac{2}{\delta}}{3(1-\gamma)}\right\}\leq\delta.

(186)

To verify this relation, we first notice that

\displaystyle|z_{k}|\leq\max_{1\leq k\leq t}\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\cdot\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\leq\frac{\eta_{t}}{1-\gamma},

(187)

provided that
\max_{k}\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\leq\eta_{t}
.
To verify the condition
\max_{k}\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\leq\eta_{t}
, one can check—similar to (
156
)—that

\frac{\eta_{k-1}\prod_{i=k}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)}{\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)}=1-\frac{\big(1-c_{\eta}(1-\gamma)\big)\eta_{k}}{1-c_{\eta}(1-\gamma)\eta_{k}}\leq 1,

(188)

which indicates that
\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)
is an increasing sequence as long as
c_{\eta}\leq\log T\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
(see (
127
)).
In addition to the boundedness condition (
187
), we can further calculate

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{t}\mathsf{Var}\big(z_{k}\,|\,P_{k-1}(1\,|\,1,1),P_{k-1}(1\,|\,1,2),\ldots,P_{1}(1\,|\,1,1),P_{1}(1\,|\,1,2)\big)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}^{2}\left[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\right]^{2}\cdot 2p(1-p)\cdot\big(\gamma\widehat{V}_{k-1}\big)^{2}\leq\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}^{2}\left[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\right]^{2}\cdot\frac{2}{3(1-\gamma)},

where the last inequality comes from the facts that
\widehat{V}_{k-1}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
and the choice
p=\frac{4\gamma-1}{3\gamma}
.
These bounds taken together with Freedman’s inequality (see (
60
)) validate (
186
).

By virtue of (
186
), setting
\delta=\frac{(1-\gamma)^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[|\zeta_{t}|^{2}\big]
yields that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle|\zeta_{t}|\leq B\coloneqq\sqrt{\frac{8\log\frac{2}{\delta}}{3(1-\gamma)}\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}^{2}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]^{2}}+\frac{4\eta_{t}\log\frac{2}{\delta}}{3(1-\gamma)}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{ with }\delta=\frac{(1-\gamma)^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[|\zeta_{t}|^{2}\big].

(189)

When
T\geq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}
, one can ensure that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\xi_{t,\mathsf{max}}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[|\zeta_{t}|\big]\geq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[|\zeta_{t}|\mathds{1}\big(|\zeta_{t}|\leq B\big)\big]\geq\frac{1}{2B}\mathbb{E}\big[|\zeta_{t}|^{2}\mathds{1}\big(|\zeta_{t}|\leq B\big)\big]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2B}\Big\{\mathbb{E}[|\zeta_{t}|^{2}]-\mathbb{E}\big[|\zeta_{t}|^{2}\mathds{1}\big(|\zeta_{t}|>B\big)\big]\Big\}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{\geq}\frac{1}{2B}\Big\{\mathbb{E}[|\zeta_{t}|^{2}]-\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\mathbb{P}\big\{|\zeta_{t}|>B\big\}\Big\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2B}\Big\{\mathbb{E}[|\zeta_{t}|^{2}]-\frac{\delta}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\Big\}\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\geq}\frac{1}{4B}\mathbb{E}[|\zeta_{t}|^{2}].

(190)

Here, (i) holds since

\displaystyle\big|\zeta_{t}\big|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{t}|z_{k}|\leq\left[\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\right]\cdot\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}

as a consequence of (
187
) and (
71
); (ii) holds by the choice of
\delta
.
It is thus sufficient to lower bound
\mathbb{E}[|\zeta_{t}|^{2}]
.
Towards this, let us define

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}\coloneqq\max\Bigg\{\tau^{\prime}\,\,\Big|\,\,\prod_{i=\tau^{\prime}}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\leq\frac{6}{7}\Bigg\},

(191)

which clearly satisfies
\tau\leq\widehat{\tau}\leq T
(in view of (
182
) and (
184
)).
Then, for all
t\geq\widehat{\tau}
one has
(which shall be proved towards the end of this subsection)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=\tau}^{t}\eta_{k}^{2}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]^{2}\geq\frac{1}{8}\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}^{2}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]^{2}.

(192)

We now proceed to lower bound
\mathbb{E}[|\zeta_{t}|^{2}]
for
t\geq\widehat{\tau}
. We first observe that for any
t\geq\widehat{\tau}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|\zeta_{t}|^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{k=1}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{Var}\Big(\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\gamma\big(P_{k}(1\,|\,1,1)-P_{k}(1\,|\,1,2)\big)\widehat{V}_{k-1}\,|\,\widehat{V}_{k-1}\Big)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=\tau}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathsf{Var}\Big(\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\gamma\big(P_{k}(1\,|\,1,1)-P_{k}(1\,|\,1,2)\big)\widehat{V}_{k-1}\,|\,\widehat{V}_{k-1}\geq\frac{1}{4(1-\gamma)}\Big)\right],

where the first line relies on (
141
),
and the last step makes use of the fact (
168
).
To further control the right-hand side of the above inequality, we take
\tau^{\prime}\coloneqq\max\left\{t-\frac{1}{\eta_{t/2}},1\right\}
and show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[|\zeta_{t}|^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{\geq}\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=\tau}^{t}\eta_{k}^{2}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]^{2}\gamma^{2}\cdot 2p(1-p)\frac{1}{16(1-\gamma)^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{48(1-\gamma)}\sum_{k=\tau}^{t}\eta_{k}^{2}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]^{2}\overset{\text{(ii)}}{\geq}\frac{1}{400(1-\gamma)}\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}^{2}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{400(1-\gamma)}\sum_{k=\tau^{\prime}}^{t}\eta_{k}^{2}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]^{2}\overset{\text{(iii)}}{\geq}\frac{\eta_{t}}{9600(1-\gamma)}.

(193)

Here, (i) makes use of the constraint
\widehat{V}_{k-1}\geq\frac{1}{4(1-\gamma)}
,
while (ii) makes use of (
192
), and (iii) are valid if the following property holds (which shall be proved towards the end of this subsection)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=\tau^{\prime}}^{t}\eta_{k}^{2}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]^{2}\geq\frac{1}{24}\eta_{t}.

(194)

We are now well-equipped to control
\mathbb{E}[\xi_{t,\mathsf{max}}]

using the property (
190
). Recall the expression of
B
in (
189
), we know that bounding
\mathbb{E}[|\zeta_{t}|^{2}]/B
boils down to controlling

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}[|\zeta_{t}|^{2}]}{\frac{\eta_{t}}{1-\gamma}\log\frac{2}{\delta}}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\frac{\mathbb{E}[|\zeta_{t}|^{2}]}{\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{2}{\delta}}{1-\gamma}\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}^{2}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]^{2}}}.

(195)

•

For the first term in (
195
), recalling that
\delta=\frac{(1-\gamma)^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[|\zeta_{t}|^{2}\big]
, we can demonstrate that

\displaystyle\log\frac{1}{\delta}=-\log\frac{(1-\gamma)^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[|\zeta_{t}|^{2}\big]\leq-\log\frac{(1-\gamma)\eta_{t}}{19200}\leq\log\frac{19200(1+(1-\gamma)T\log T)}{1-\gamma}\lesssim\log T,

(196)

where the first inequality makes use of the bound (
193
), and the second inequality arises from the fact
\eta_{t}\geq\frac{1}{1+(1-\gamma)T\log T}
(given the range of the learning rates in this case).
Combining this with (
193
),
we can guarantee that

\frac{\mathbb{E}[|\zeta_{t}|^{2}]}{\frac{\eta_{t}}{1-\gamma}\log\frac{2}{\delta}}\gtrsim\frac{1}{\log T}.

•

Moving to the second term in (
195
), one can ensure that

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}\big[|\zeta_{t}|^{2}\big]}{\sqrt{\frac{\log\frac{2}{\delta}}{1-\gamma}\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}^{2}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]^{2}}}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\gtrsim}\sqrt{\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)\log T}\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}^{2}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]^{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{\gtrsim}\sqrt{\frac{\eta_{t}}{(1-\gamma)\log T}}\overset{\mathrm{(iii)}}{\gtrsim}\frac{1}{\sqrt{(1-\gamma)^{2}T}\log T}.

Here, (i) follows from (
193
) and (
196
) since

\mathbb{E}\big[|\zeta_{t}|^{2}\big]\gtrsim\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}^{2}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]^{2}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\log\frac{2}{\delta}\lesssim\log T;

(ii) arises from (
194
); and (iii) relies on the fact
\eta_{t}\gtrsim\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)T\log T}
(given the range of the learning rates in this case).

Substituting the above relations into (
190
) and using the expression of
B
in (
189
), we reach at

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\xi_{t,\mathsf{max}}]\geq\frac{1}{4B}\mathbb{E}\big[|\zeta_{t}|^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c}{\sqrt{(1-\gamma)^{2}T}\log T},

for some constant
c>0
. Thus, this validates the inequality (
175
).

Proof of the claim (
192
).

By the definition of
\widehat{\tau}
in (
191
), we have

\prod_{i=\widehat{\tau}}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\leq 6/7
. An important observation is that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=\tau}^{t}\eta_{k}\left[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{(i)}}}{{=}}1-\prod_{i=\tau}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{(ii)}}}{{\geq}}\frac{1}{8}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{(iii)}}}{{\geq}}\frac{1}{8}\sum_{k=1}^{t}\eta_{k}\left[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\right].

(197)

Here, the relations (i) and (iii) arise from (
72
), and the inequality (ii) follows since

\displaystyle\prod_{i=\tau}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\leq\prod_{i=\tau}^{\widehat{\tau}}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\leq\prod_{i=\tau}^{\widehat{\tau}}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)=\frac{\prod_{i=\tau}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)}{\prod_{i=\widehat{\tau}+1}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)}\leq\frac{\,3/4\,}{6/7}\leq\frac{7}{8},

(198)

where
\tau
is defined in (
181
) and (
183
) for linearly rescaled learning rates and

\tau=1
for constant learning rates, and
we have also made use of (
161
), (
182
) and (
184
) in the penultimate inequality in (
198
).

With (
197
) in place, we can continue to prove the claim (
192
). Recognizing that
\eta_{k}\big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\big]
is increasing in
k
(see (
188
)),
we can obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\tau-1}\eta_{k}^{2}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq k<\tau}\left\{\eta_{k}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]\right\}\sum_{k=1}^{\tau-1}\eta_{k}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\eta_{\tau}\Big[\prod_{i=\tau+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]\sum_{k=1}^{\tau-1}\eta_{k}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 7\eta_{\tau}\Big[\prod_{i=\tau+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]\sum_{k=\tau}^{t}\eta_{k}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big],

(199)

where the last inequality comes from (
197
).
With the preceding inequality in place, the claim (
192
) then follows by observing that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=\tau}^{t}\eta_{k}^{2}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\min_{\tau\leq k\leq t}\left\{\eta_{k}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]\right\}\sum_{k=\tau}^{t}\eta_{k}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\geq\eta_{\tau}\Big[\prod_{i=\tau+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]\sum_{k=\tau}^{t}\eta_{k}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big],

where we make use of the monotonicity of
\eta_{k}\big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\big]
again.
∎

Proof of the claim (
194
).

Note that for
\tau^{\prime}\coloneqq\max\left\{t-\frac{1}{\eta_{t/2}},1\right\}
, one has

\eta_{k}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]\geq\eta_{t}\big(1-\eta_{t/2}\big)^{t-\tau^{\prime}}\geq\eta_{t}\big(1-\eta_{t/2}\big)^{1/\eta_{t/2}}\geq\frac{1}{3}\eta_{t},\qquad\text{for all}\penalty\ \tau^{\prime}\leq k\leq t,

as long as the following condition holds (recalling the definition of
\widehat{\tau}
in (
191
))

\eta_{t/2}\leq 2\eta_{t}\leq 2\eta_{\widehat{\tau}}\leq 1/10.

(200)

In addition,
similar to (
131
), we can derive

\displaystyle\sum_{k=\tau^{\prime}}^{t}\eta_{k}\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)

\displaystyle=1-\prod_{i=\tau^{\prime}}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\geq 1-\max\left\{\big(1-\eta_{t}\big)^{1/\eta_{t/2}+1},\,\prod_{i=1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\right\}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\max\left\{e^{-1/2},\,\prod_{i=1}^{\widehat{\tau}}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\right\}\geq\frac{1}{8},

where we once again use the condition (
200
), and the last inequality comes from the derivation in (
198
).
Putting these two bounds together yields

\displaystyle\sum_{k=\tau^{\prime}}^{t}\eta_{k}^{2}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\min_{k:\,\tau^{\prime}\leq k\leq t}\left\{\eta_{k}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]\right\}\sum_{k=\tau^{\prime}}^{t}\eta_{k}\Big[\prod_{i=k+1}^{t}\big(1-\eta_{i}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{3}\eta_{t}\cdot\frac{1}{8}\geq\frac{1}{24}\eta_{t}.

To finish up, it remains to justify (
200
).
This condition is obvious for constant learning rates. As for rescaled learning rates, one can see that

\eta_{i}=\frac{1}{1+(1-\gamma)c_{\eta}i}\geq\frac{19}{20c_{\eta}(1-\gamma)i}\qquad\text{for all }i\geq\overline{\tau},

where
\overline{\tau}\coloneqq\lceil\frac{19}{c_{\eta}(1-\gamma)}\rceil
. This allows one to obtain

\log\Big[\prod_{i=\overline{\tau}}^{T}\big(1-\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\big)\Big]\leq-\sum_{i=\overline{\tau}}^{T}\eta_{i}(1-\gamma p)\leq-\sum_{i=\overline{\tau}}^{T}\frac{19}{15c_{\eta}i}\leq-\frac{19\log\frac{\,T\,}{\overline{\tau}}}{15c_{\eta}}\leq-\frac{19\log\frac{c_{\eta}(1-\gamma)T}{20}}{15c_{\eta}}\leq-\frac{1}{5},

provided that
T\geq\frac{c_{1}}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}
for some sufficiently large constant
c_{1}>0
and
1-\gamma<c_{\eta}<\log T
.
Taking this together with (
191
) implies that
\widehat{\tau}\geq\overline{\tau}
and hence

\eta_{\widehat{\tau}}\leq\eta_{\overline{\tau}}=\frac{1}{1+(1-\gamma)c_{\eta}\overline{\tau}\,}=1/20
.
∎

D.4
Proof of Lemma
3

Given that state
0
is an absorbing state with zero immediate reward, it is easily seen that

V^{\pi}(0)=0\qquad\text{for all }\pi\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad V^{\star}(0)=Q^{\star}(0,1)=0.

Moreover, by construction, taking action 1 and taking action 2 in state 1 result
in the same behavior (in terms of both the reward function and the
associated transition probability), and as a consequence,

Q^{\star}(1,1)=Q^{\star}(1,2)=V^{\star}(1).

(201)

From Bellman’s equation, we can thus deduce that

Q^{\star}(1,1)=r(1,1)+\gamma P(0\,|\,1,1)V^{\star}(0)+\gamma P(1\,|\,1,1)V^{\star}(1),

which in conjunction with (
201
) and a little algebra leads to

V^{\star}(1)=\frac{r(1,1)+\gamma P(0\,|\,1,1)V^{\star}(0)}{1-\gamma P(1\,|\,1,1)}=\frac{1}{1-\gamma p}=\frac{3}{4(1-\gamma)}.

Here, the second identity follows since
V^{\star}(0)=0
, and the
third identity makes use of (
45
).
The calculation for
V^{\star}(2)
and
Q^{\star}(2,1)
follows from an identical argument and is hence omitted.

Turning to state
3
, by Bellman’s equation, we have

V^{\star}(3)=Q^{\star}(3,1)=r(3,1)+\gamma P(3\,|\,3,1)V^{\star}(3)=1+\gamma V^{\star}(3),

which leads to
V^{\star}(3)=\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
.

Appendix E
Analysis for asynchronous Q-learning (Theorem
4
)

E.1
Notation and preliminary facts

Vector and matrix notation.

We shall adopt the vector notation
\bm{Q}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
,
\bm{V}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}|}
,
\bm{r}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
in the same way as in Section
4.1
.
The sample transition matrix
\bm{P}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|\times|\mathcal{S}|}
in the asynchronous case is defined such that

\bm{P}_{t}\big((s,a),s^{\prime}\big)=\begin{cases}1,\quad&\text{if }(s,a,s^{\prime})=(s_{t-1},a_{t-1},s_{t});\\
0,\quad&\text{else}.\end{cases}

(202)

It is also handy to introduce the diagonal matrix

\bm{\Lambda}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|\times|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|}
such that

\bm{\Lambda}_{t}\big((s,a),(s,a)\big)=\begin{cases}\eta,\qquad&\text{if }(s,a)=(s_{t-1},a_{t-1});\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

(203)

Armed with the above notation, the asynchronous Q-learning update rule (
50
) can be conveniently expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\bm{Q}_{t}=\big(\bm{I}-\bm{\Lambda}_{t}\big)\bm{Q}_{t-1}+\bm{\Lambda}_{t}\big(\bm{r}+\gamma\bm{P}_{t}\bm{V}_{t-1}\big).

(204)

Range of
V_{t}
and
Q_{t}
.

Similar to the synchronous counterpart, we have the following elementary properties.

Lemma 7
.

Suppose that
0<\eta_{t}\leq 1
for all
t\geq 0
. Assume that
\bm{0}\leq\bm{Q}_{0}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\bm{1}
.
Then for any
t\geq 0
, one has

\displaystyle\bm{0}\leq\bm{Q}_{t}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\bm{1}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\bm{0}\leq\bm{V}_{t}\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\bm{1}.

(205)

Proof.

The proof is the same as that of Lemma
4
, and is hence omitted for brevity. ∎

E.2
Main steps for proving Theorem
4

We are now in a position to outline the main steps for the proof of Theorem
4
.

Step 1: deriving basic recursions.

According to the update rule (
204
), we can derive the following elementary decomposition

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{t}\coloneqq\bm{Q}_{t}-\bm{Q}^{\star}

\displaystyle=\big(\bm{I}-\bm{\Lambda}_{t}\big)\bm{Q}_{t-1}+\bm{\Lambda}_{t}\big(\bm{r}+\gamma\bm{P}_{t}\bm{V}_{t-1}\big)-\bm{Q}^{\star}

\displaystyle=\big(\bm{I}-\bm{\Lambda}_{t}\big)\big(\bm{Q}_{t-1}-\bm{Q}^{\star}\big)+\bm{\Lambda}_{t}\big(\bm{r}+\gamma\bm{P}_{t}\bm{V}_{t-1}-\bm{Q}^{\star}\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\bm{I}-\bm{\Lambda}_{t}\big)\big(\bm{Q}_{t-1}-\bm{Q}^{\star}\big)+\gamma\bm{\Lambda}_{t}\big(\bm{P}_{t}\bm{V}_{t-1}-\bm{P}\bm{V}^{\star}\big)

\displaystyle=\big(\bm{I}-\bm{\Lambda}_{t}\big)\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}+\gamma\bm{\Lambda}_{t}\big(\bm{P}_{t}-\bm{P}\big)\bm{V}_{t-1}+\gamma\bm{\Lambda}_{t}\bm{P}\big(\bm{V}_{t-1}-\bm{V}^{\star}\big),

(206)

where the penultimate identity follows from the Bellman optimality equation
\bm{Q}^{\star}=\bm{r}+\bm{P}\bm{V}^{\star}
.
Combining (
206
) with the inequalities (
33
) and using the definition (
27
) of
\pi_{t}
result in

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\big(\bm{I}-\bm{\Lambda}_{t}\big)\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}+\gamma\bm{\Lambda}_{t}\big(\bm{P}_{t}-\bm{P}\big)\bm{V}_{t-1}+\gamma\bm{\Lambda}_{t}\bm{P}^{\pi_{t-1}}\bm{\Delta}_{t-1},

(207a)

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{t}

\displaystyle\geq\big(\bm{I}-\bm{\Lambda}_{t}\big)\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}+\gamma\bm{\Lambda}_{t}\big(\bm{P}_{t}-\bm{P}\big)\bm{V}_{t-1}+\gamma\bm{\Lambda}_{t}\bm{P}^{\pi^{\star}}\bm{\Delta}_{t-1}.

(207b)

Apply the above two relations recursively to obtain

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\sum_{i=1}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\bm{\Lambda}_{j}\big)\bm{\Lambda}_{i}\big(\bm{P}_{i}-\bm{P}\big)\bm{V}_{i-1}+\gamma\sum_{i=1}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\bm{\Lambda}_{j}\big)\bm{\Lambda}_{i}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i-1}}\bm{\Delta}_{i-1}+\prod_{j=1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\bm{\Lambda}_{j}\big)\bm{\Delta}_{0},

(208a)

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{t}

\displaystyle\geq\gamma\sum_{i=1}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\bm{\Lambda}_{j}\big)\bm{\Lambda}_{i}\big(\bm{P}_{i}-\bm{P}\big)\bm{V}_{i-1}+\gamma\sum_{i=1}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\bm{\Lambda}_{j}\big)\bm{\Lambda}_{i}\bm{P}^{\pi^{\star}}\bm{\Delta}_{i-1}+\prod_{j=1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\bm{\Lambda}_{j}\big)\bm{\Delta}_{0}.

(208b)

By defining the following diagonal matrices

\bm{\Lambda}_{i}^{(t)}\coloneqq\begin{cases}\prod_{j=1}^{t}(\bm{I}-\bm{\Lambda}_{j}),&\text{if }i=0,\\
\bm{\Lambda}_{i}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}(\bm{I}-\bm{\Lambda}_{j}),&\text{if }0<i<t,\\
\bm{\Lambda}_{t},&\text{if }i=t,\end{cases}

(209)

and setting

\beta=\frac{c_{3}(1-\gamma)}{\log T}

(210)

for some constant
c_{3}>0
,
we can rearrange terms in the upper bound (
208a
) to reach

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\underset{\eqqcolon\,\bm{\zeta}_{t}}{\underbrace{\bm{\Lambda}_{0}^{(t)}\bm{\Delta}_{0}+\gamma\sum_{i=1}^{(1-\beta)t}\bm{\Lambda}_{i}^{(t)}\Big[\big(\bm{P}_{i}-\bm{P}\big)\bm{V}_{i-1}+\bm{P}^{\pi_{i-1}}\bm{\Delta}_{i-1}\Big]}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underset{\eqqcolon\,\bm{\xi}_{t}}{\underbrace{\gamma\sum_{i=(1-\beta)t+1}^{t}\bm{\Lambda}_{i}^{(t)}\big(\bm{P}_{i}-\bm{P}\big)\bm{V}_{i-1}}}+\gamma\sum_{i=(1-\beta)t+1}^{t}\bm{\Lambda}_{i}^{(t)}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i-1}}\bm{\Delta}_{i-1}.

(211)

In the subsequent steps, we shall first develop bounds on the sizes of the terms
\bm{\zeta}_{t}
and
\bm{\xi}_{t}
in (
211
) separately, and then combine these bounds with (
211
) recursively in order to derive the advertised upper bound on
\bm{\Delta}_{t}
.

Step 2: bounding the terms
\bm{\zeta}_{t}
and
\bm{\xi}_{t}
.

The terms
\bm{\zeta}_{t}
and
\bm{\xi}_{t}
defined in (
211
) can be bounded with high probability by the following lemmas.

Lemma 8
.

With probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\|\bm{\zeta}_{t}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{4}{(1-\gamma)T}

(212)

for all
t
obeying
\frac{T}{c_{4}\log T}\leq t\leq T
. Here,
c_{4}>0
is some constant obeying
c_{4}\leq c_{1}c_{3}/4
,
where the constants
c_{1}
and
c_{3}
appear in (
54a
) and (
210
), respectively.

Proof.

See Section
E.3.1
. ∎

Lemma 9
.

Suppose that
0<\eta\leq\frac{\log^{3}T}{(1-\gamma)T\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}
. With probability at least
1-\delta
, one has

\displaystyle|\bm{\xi}_{t}|\leq\sqrt{\frac{16\big(\log^{3}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)T\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}\Big(\max_{(1-\beta)t\leq i<t}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}(\bm{V}_{i})+\bm{1}\Big)}+\frac{6\big(\log^{3}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)^{2}T\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}\bm{1}

(213)

for all
t
obeying
\frac{T}{c_{4}\log T}\leq t\leq T
for some constant
c_{4}>0
.

Proof.

See Section
E.3.2
. ∎

Step 3: controlling
\bm{\Delta}_{t}
.

Consider any
t
obeying
\frac{T}{c_{4}\log T}\leq t\leq T
and any
k
obeying
2t/3\leq k\leq t
.
Under the sample size condition (
54b
), Lemmas
8
-
9
together with a little algebra lead to

|\bm{\zeta}_{k}|+|\bm{\xi}_{k}|\leq\sqrt{\frac{32\big(\log^{3}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)T\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}\bigg(\max_{(1-\beta)k\leq i\leq k}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}(\bm{V}_{i})+\bm{1}\bigg)}\leq\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}},

where we define

\bm{\varphi}_{t}\coloneqq\frac{32\big(\log^{3}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)T\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}\bigg(\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i\leq t}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}(\bm{V}_{i})+\bm{1}\bigg).

(214)

Combining this inequality with (
211
) allows us to obtain

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}+\sum_{i=(1-\beta)k+1}^{k}\bm{\Lambda}_{i}^{(k)}\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i-1}}\bm{\Delta}_{i-1}=\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}+\sum_{i=(1-\beta)k}^{k-1}\bm{\Lambda}_{i+1}^{(k)}\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i}}\bm{\Delta}_{i}\qquad\text{for all }\penalty\ 2t/3\leq k\leq t.

(215)

Similar to the quantity
\alpha_{i}^{(t)}
defined in (
78
), let us define

\displaystyle\bm{D}_{i}^{(t)}\coloneqq\bigg(\sum_{j=(1-\beta)t}^{t-1}\bm{\Lambda}_{j+1}^{(t)}\bigg)^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}_{i+1}^{(t)},

(216)

which, according to (
71
) and the definition (
203
), clearly satisfies

\bm{D}_{i}^{(t)}\geq\bm{\Lambda}_{i+1}^{(t)}\geq\bm{0}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\sum_{i=(1-\beta)t}^{t-1}\bm{D}_{i}^{(t)}=\bm{I}.

(217)

Set
i_{0}=t
for notational convenience. With this set of notation and the property (
217
) in mind, we can derive the following bound

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i_{1}=(1-\beta)t}^{t-1}\Big(\bm{D}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}+\bm{\Lambda}_{i_{1}+1}^{(t)}\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{1}}}\bm{\Delta}_{i_{1}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i_{1}=(1-\beta)t}^{t-1}\Bigg[\bm{D}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}+\bm{\Lambda}_{i_{1}+1}^{(t)}\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{1}}}\sum_{i_{2}=(1-\beta)i_{1}}^{i_{1}-1}\Big(\bm{D}_{i_{2}}^{(i_{1})}\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}+\bm{\Lambda}_{i_{2}+1}^{(i_{1})}\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{2}}}\bm{\Delta}_{i_{2}}\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i_{1}=(1-\beta)t}^{t-1}\bm{D}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}+\sum_{i_{1}=(1-\beta)t}^{t-1}\bm{D}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\sum_{i_{2}=(1-\beta)i_{1}}^{i_{1}-1}\big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{1}}}\bm{D}_{i_{2}}^{(i_{1})}\big)\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\sum_{i_{1}=(1-\beta)t}^{t-1}\sum_{i_{2}=(1-\beta)i_{1}}^{i_{1}-1}\prod_{k=1}^{2}\big(\bm{\Lambda}_{i_{k}+1}^{(i_{k-1})}\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\big)\bm{\Delta}_{i_{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1}=(1-\beta)t}^{t-1}\bm{D}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\Bigg\{\bm{I}+\sum_{i_{2}=(1-\beta)i_{1}}^{i_{1}-1}\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{1}}}\bm{D}_{i_{2}}^{(i_{1})}\Bigg\}\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}+\sum_{i_{1}=(1-\beta)t}^{t-1}\sum_{i_{2}=(1-\beta)i_{1}}^{i_{1}-1}\prod_{k=1}^{2}\big(\bm{\Lambda}_{i_{k}+1}^{(i_{k-1})}\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\big)\bm{\Delta}_{i_{2}}.

(218)

Here, the first relation makes use of the second property in (
217
),
the second relation further expands
\bm{\Delta}_{i_{1}}
in the same way as in the first line of (
218
),
whereas the third inequality relies on the first property in (
217
).

Next, we intend to invoke the above relation multiple times to reach a simpler relation.
Set

\displaystyle H\coloneqq\frac{\log T}{1-\gamma}.

(219)

Similar to the way we derive (
86
), we can apply the relation (
218
) recursively and use the basic relation
|\bm{\Delta}_{k}|\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\bm{1}
for any
k
to show that

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\left(i_{1},\cdots,i_{H}\right)\in\mathcal{I}_{t}}\Bigg\{\bm{D}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\bigg(\bm{I}+\sum_{h=1}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\big(\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bm{D}_{i_{k+1}}^{(i_{k})}\big)\bigg)\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}+\gamma^{H}\prod_{k=1}^{H}\big(\bm{\Lambda}_{i_{k}+1}^{(i_{k-1})}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\big)\big|\bm{\Delta}_{i_{H}}\big|\Bigg\},

(220)

where
\mathcal{I}_{t}
has been defined in (
84
). To further simplify (
220
),
we need to control the two terms on the right-hand side of (
220
) separately.

•

We shall begin with the first term on the right-hand side of (
220
). Towards this end, let us define a collection of policies
\{\widehat{\pi}_{k}\}
recursively and backward as follows:

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}_{k}\coloneqq\begin{cases}\arg\max_{\pi\in\Pi}\bm{\Pi}^{\pi}\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}},&\text{if }k=H-1,\\
\arg\max_{\pi\in\Pi}\bm{\Pi}^{\pi}\Big(\bm{I}+\sum_{h=k+1}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\prod_{j=1}^{h}\bm{P}^{\widehat{\pi}_{j}}\Big)\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}},\quad&\text{if }k=H-2,\cdots,1,\end{cases}

(221)

or alternatively (in view of the definition (
24
) of
\bm{P}^{\pi}
),

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}_{k}\coloneqq\begin{cases}\arg\max_{\pi\in\Pi}\bm{P}^{\pi}\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}},&\text{if }k=H-1;\\
\arg\max_{\pi\in\Pi}\bm{P}^{\pi}\Big(\bm{I}+\sum_{h=k+1}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\prod_{j=1}^{h}\bm{P}^{\widehat{\pi}_{j}}\Big)\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}},\quad&\text{if }k=H-2,\cdots,1.\end{cases}

(222)

Here,
\Pi
is a policy set satisfying

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Pi\coloneqq\big\{\pi=[\pi(s)]_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\,\big|\,\pi(s)\in\Pi_{s},\forall s\in\mathcal{S}\big\},\qquad\Pi_{s}\coloneqq\big\{\pi_{i}(s)\mid i\in[t/2,t)\big\};\end{aligned}

(223)

in words, for any policy
\pi
belonging to
\Pi
, each
\pi(s)
coincides with one of the policy iterates
\pi_{i}(s)
during the latest
\beta t
iterations, although we do not require all
\{\pi(s)\}
across different states to be associated with the same time stamp
i
.
With this collection of policies in place, we can deduce that

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{H}}\bm{D}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\bigg(\bm{I}+\sum_{h=1}^{H-1}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bm{D}_{i_{k+1}}^{(i_{k})}\big)\bigg)\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{H-1}}\sum_{i_{H}}\bm{D}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\bigg(\bm{I}+\sum_{h=1}^{H-2}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bm{D}_{i_{k+1}}^{(i_{k})}\big)+\prod_{k=1}^{H-2}\big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bm{D}_{i_{k+1}}^{(i_{k})}\big)\Big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{H-1}}}\bm{D}_{i_{H}}^{(i_{H-1})}\Big)\bigg)\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{H-1}}\bm{D}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\bigg(\bm{I}+\sum_{h=1}^{H-2}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bm{D}_{i_{k+1}}^{(i_{k})}\big)+\prod_{k=1}^{H-2}\big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bm{D}_{i_{k+1}}^{(i_{k})}\big)\Big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{H-1}}}\sum_{i_{H}}\bm{D}_{i_{H}}^{(i_{H-1})}\Big)\bigg)\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{=}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{H-1}}\bm{D}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\bigg(\bm{I}+\sum_{h=1}^{H-2}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bm{D}_{i_{k+1}}^{(i_{k})}\big)+\prod_{k=1}^{H-2}\big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bm{D}_{i_{k+1}}^{(i_{k})}\big)\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{H-1}}}\bigg)\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\leq}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{H-1}}\bm{D}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\bigg(\bm{I}+\sum_{h=1}^{H-2}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bm{D}_{i_{k+1}}^{(i_{k})}\big)+\prod_{k=1}^{H-2}\big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bm{D}_{i_{k+1}}^{(i_{k})}\big)\gamma\bm{P}^{\widehat{\pi}_{H-1}}\bigg)\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{H-2}}\bm{D}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\bigg(\bm{I}+\sum_{h=1}^{H-3}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bm{D}_{i_{k+1}}^{(i_{k})}\big)+\prod_{k=1}^{H-3}\big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bm{D}_{i_{k+1}}^{(i_{k})}\big)\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{H-2}}}\bigg(\sum_{i_{H-1}}\bm{D}_{i_{H-1}}^{(i_{H-2})}\bigg)\big(\bm{I}+\gamma\bm{P}^{\widehat{\pi}_{H-1}}\big)\bigg)\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{iii})}{=}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{H-2}}\bm{D}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\bigg(\bm{I}+\sum_{h=1}^{H-3}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bm{D}_{i_{k+1}}^{(i_{k})}\big)+\prod_{k=1}^{H-3}\big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bm{D}_{i_{k+1}}^{(i_{k})}\big)\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{H-2}}}\big(\bm{I}+\gamma\bm{P}^{\widehat{\pi}_{H-1}}\big)\bigg)\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{iv})}{\leq}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{H-2}}\bm{D}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\bigg(\bm{I}+\sum_{h=1}^{H-3}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bm{D}_{i_{k+1}}^{(i_{k})}\big)+\prod_{k=1}^{H-3}\big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bm{D}_{i_{k+1}}^{(i_{k})}\big)\gamma\bm{P}^{\widehat{\pi}_{H-2}}\big(\bm{I}+\gamma\bm{P}^{\widehat{\pi}_{H-1}}\big)\bigg)\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}},

where we abbreviate
\sum_{(i_{1},\cdots,i_{H})\in\mathcal{I}_{t}}
as
\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{H}}

as long as it is clear from the context. Here, (i) and (iii) arise from the second property in (
217
),
while (ii) and (iv) are due to the construction (
222
).
Continuing the derivation of the above inequality recursively, we arrive at

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{H}}\bm{D}_{i_{1}}^{(t)}\bigg(\bm{I}+\sum_{h=1}^{H-1}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\big(\gamma\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\bm{D}_{i_{k+1}}^{(i_{k})}\big)\bigg)\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq\bigg(\bm{I}+\sum_{h=1}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\bm{P}^{\widehat{\pi}_{k}}\bigg)\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}.

•

We now turn attention to the second term on the right-hand side of (
220
). It is seen that

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{H}}\prod_{k=1}^{H}\gamma^{H}\big(\bm{\Lambda}_{i_{k}+1}^{(i_{k-1})}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\big)\big|\bm{\Delta}_{i_{H}}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\gamma^{H}}{1-\gamma}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{H}}\prod_{k=1}^{H}\big(\bm{D}_{i_{k}}^{(i_{k-1})}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\big)\bm{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{H}}{1-\gamma}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{H-1}}\prod_{k=1}^{H-1}\big(\bm{D}_{i_{k}}^{(i_{k-1})}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\big)\bigg(\sum_{i_{H}}\bm{D}_{i_{H}}^{(i_{H-1})}\big(\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{H}}}\bm{1}\big)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{H}}{1-\gamma}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{H-1}}\prod_{k=1}^{H-1}\big(\bm{D}_{i_{k}}^{(i_{k-1})}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\big)\bigg(\sum_{i_{H}}\bm{D}_{i_{H}}^{(i_{H-1})}\bigg)\bm{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma^{H}}{1-\gamma}\sum_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{H-1}}\prod_{k=1}^{H-1}\big(\bm{D}_{i_{k}}^{(i_{k-1})}\bm{P}^{\pi_{i_{k}}}\big)\bm{1}

\displaystyle=\cdots=\frac{\gamma^{H}}{1-\gamma}\bm{1}=\frac{\gamma^{\frac{\log T}{1-\gamma}}}{1-\gamma}\bm{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)T}\bm{1},

where the first line follows from the first property in (
217
),
the third line is due to the fact
\bm{P}^{\pi}\bm{1}=\bm{1}
for any
\pi
,
and the fourth line arises from the second property in (
217
).

Substituting the above two bounds into (
220
) yields

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\bigg(\bm{I}+\sum_{h=1}^{H-1}\gamma^{h}\prod_{k=1}^{h}\bm{P}^{\widehat{\pi}_{k}}\bigg)\sqrt{\bm{\varphi}_{t}}}_{\eqqcolon\,\bm{\beta}}+\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)T}\bm{1}.

(224)

Step 4: putting all pieces together.

Repeating our analysis for the term
\bm{\beta}_{1}
in Section
B.2
(i.e., Step 5 of Section
B.2
with Lemma
5
replaced by Lemma
6
),
we arrive at

\displaystyle|\bm{\beta}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{320\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T}\Big(1+2\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\Big)\bm{1}

with probability at least
1-\delta
.
Substitution into (
224
) then yields

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{320\big(\log^{3}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}\Big(1+\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\Big)}\ \bm{1}+\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)T}\bm{1}

\displaystyle\leq 30\sqrt{\frac{\big(\log^{3}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}\Big(1+\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\Big)}\ \bm{1}

(225)

holds simultaneously for all
t\geq\frac{T}{c_{4}\log T}
, provided that the sample size condition (
54b
) is satisfied.
Similarly, we can also establish the following lower bound on
\bm{\Delta}_{t}
(which we omit the details for the sake of brevity)

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}_{t}\geq-30\sqrt{\frac{\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}\Big(1+\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\Big)}\ \bm{1}

with probability at least
1-\delta
. To summarize, it is seen that with probability exceeding
1-2\delta
,

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}_{t}\|_{\infty}\leq 30\sqrt{\frac{\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{\gamma^{2}(1-\gamma)^{4}T\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}\Big(1+\max_{\frac{t}{2}\leq i<t}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\|_{\infty}\Big)}.

(226)

This resembles the relation (
42
) derived for the synchronous case,
except that
T
in the denominator is replaced with
\mu_{\mathsf{min}}T
.
As a result, we can readily repeat the argument in Appendix
B.4
to reach

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}_{T}\|_{\infty}\leq O\bigg(\sqrt{\frac{\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)^{4}T\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}}+\frac{\big(\log^{4}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)^{4}T\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}\bigg),

(227)

which in turn establishes the claimed result in Theorem
4
.

E.3
Proofs of technical lemmas

E.3.1
Proof of Lemma
8

In view of the definition of
\bm{\zeta}_{t}
in (
211
) and the fact that
\bm{\Lambda}_{0}^{(t)}
is a diagonal matrix,
we can deduce that

\displaystyle\|\bm{\zeta}_{t}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{\Lambda}_{0}^{(t)}\|\|\bm{\Delta}_{0}\|_{\infty}+t\max_{1\leq i\leq(1-\beta)t}\|\bm{\Lambda}_{i}^{(t)}\|\max_{1\leq i\leq(1-\beta)t}\big(\|\bm{P}^{\pi_{i-1}}\bm{\Delta}_{i-1}\|_{\infty}+\|\bm{P}_{i}\bm{V}_{i-1}\|_{\infty}+\|\bm{P}\bm{V}_{i-1}\|_{\infty}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{\Lambda}_{0}^{(t)}\|\|\bm{\Delta}_{0}\|_{\infty}+t\max_{1\leq i\leq(1-\beta)t}\|\bm{\Lambda}_{i}^{(t)}\|\max_{1\leq i\leq(1-\beta)t}\Big\{\|\bm{P}^{\pi_{i-1}}\|_{1}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i-1}\|_{\infty}+\big(\|\bm{P}_{i}\|_{1}+\|\bm{P}\|_{1}\big)\|\bm{V}_{i-1}\|_{\infty}\Big\}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{=}\|\bm{\Lambda}_{0}^{(t)}\|\|\bm{\Delta}_{0}\|_{\infty}+t\max_{1\leq i\leq(1-\beta)t}\|\bm{\Lambda}_{i}^{(t)}\|\max_{1\leq i\leq(1-\beta)t}\big(\|\bm{\Delta}_{i-1}\|_{\infty}+2\,\|\bm{V}_{i-1}\|_{\infty}\big)

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\leq}\frac{1}{T^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{1-\gamma}+\frac{1}{T^{2}}\cdot t\cdot\frac{3}{1-\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4}{(1-\gamma)T}.

Here, (i) holds true since
\|\bm{P}^{\pi_{i-1}}\|_{1}=\|\bm{P}_{i}\|_{1}=\|\bm{P}\|_{1}=1
. To verify (ii), we first define

t_{k}(s,a)\coloneqq\text{the time stamp when }\text{the trajectory visits }(s,a)\text{ for the }k\text{-th time}

(228)

and

K_{t}(s,a)\coloneqq\Big|\big\{k\geq 1\mid t_{k}(s,a)<t\big\}\Big|,

(229)

namely, the total number of times — before the
t
-th iteration —
that the sample trajectory visits
(s,a)
.
Then
Li et al., 2022c
tells us that with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle K_{t_{1}}(s,a)-K_{t_{2}}(s,a)\geq\frac{1}{2}(t_{1}-t_{2})\mu_{\mathsf{min}},

(230)

holds uniformly for all
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
and
0\leq t_{2}\leq t_{1}\leq T
obeying

t_{1}-t_{2}\geq\frac{886t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}.

This in turn implies that:
if
\beta t\geq\frac{886t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}
and
i\leq(1-\beta)t
, then one has

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Lambda}_{0}^{(t)}\|

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\prod_{j=1}^{t}\big(\bm{I}-\bm{\Lambda}_{j}\big)\bigg\|=\max_{(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}}(1-\eta)^{K_{t}(s,a)}\leq(1-\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}t\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}\leq\frac{1}{T^{2}}

(231a)

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Lambda}_{i}^{(t)}\|

\displaystyle=\bigg\|\bm{\Lambda}_{i}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}(\bm{I}-\bm{\Lambda}_{j})\bigg\|\leq\max_{(s,a)}(1-\eta)^{K_{t}(s,a)-K_{i}(s,a)}\leq(1-\eta)^{\frac{1}{2}\beta t\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}\leq\frac{1}{T^{2}}

(231b)

with probability at least
1-\delta
,
provided that
\eta\beta t\mu_{\mathsf{min}}>4\log T
.
In other words, (
231
) holds with probability at least
1-\delta
, as long as

t>\max\left\{\frac{4\log T}{\eta\beta\mu_{\mathsf{min}}},\,\frac{886t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{\beta\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\right\}=\max\left\{\frac{4T}{c_{1}c_{3}\log T},\,\frac{886t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{c_{3}(1-\gamma)\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\log T\right\}.

This taken together with the sample size assumption (
54b
) concludes the proof of Lemma
8
.

E.3.2
Proof of Lemma
9

Fix any state-action pair
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
, and let us look at the
(s,a)
-th entry of
\bm{\xi}_{t}
, i.e.,

\xi_{t}(s,a)
.
For notational simplicity, let
\bm{\Lambda}_{j}(s,a)
denote the
(s,a)
-th diagonal entry
of the diagonal matrix
\bm{\Lambda}_{j}
, and
\bm{P}_{t}(s,a)

(resp.
\bm{P}(s,a)
) the
(s,a)
-th row of
\bm{P}_{t}
(resp.
\bm{P}
).

Using the definition of
\bm{\xi}_{t}
in (
211
) and the above notation, we can derive

\displaystyle\xi_{t}(s,a)

\displaystyle=\gamma\sum_{i=(1-\beta)t+1}^{t}\prod_{j=i+1}^{t}\big(1-\bm{\Lambda}_{j}(s,a)\big)\bm{\Lambda}_{i}(s,a)\big(\bm{P}_{i}(s,a)-\bm{P}(s,a)\big)\bm{V}_{i-1}.

(232)

Equipped with the definitions of
t_{k}(s,a)
(cf. (
228
)) and
K_{t}(s,a)
(cf. (
229
)),
we can further rewrite (
232
) as

\displaystyle\xi_{t}(s,a)

\displaystyle=\gamma\sum_{k=K_{(1-\beta)t+1}}^{K_{t}(s,a)}(1-\eta)^{K_{t}(s,a)-k}\eta\big(\bm{P}_{t_{k}+1}(s,a)-\bm{P}(s,a)\big)\bm{V}_{t_{k}}.

(233)

In what follows, we shall suppress the notation and write
t_{k}=t_{k}(s,a)

and
K_{t}=K_{t}(s,a)
to streamline notation.

The main step thus boils down to controlling (
233
).
Towards this, we claim that: with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\Bigg|\sum_{k=K_{\beta}}^{K}(1-\eta)^{K-k}\eta\big(\bm{P}_{t_{k}+1}(s,a)-\bm{P}(s,a)\big)\bm{V}_{t_{k}}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sqrt{\frac{16\big(\log^{3}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)T\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}\Big(\max_{t_{K_{\beta}}\leq i\leq t_{K}}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}(s,a)}(\bm{V}_{i})+1\Big)}+\frac{6\big(\log^{3}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)^{2}T\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}

(234)

holds simultaneously for all
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
and
all
1\leq K_{\beta}\leq K\leq T
, provided that
0<\eta\leq\frac{\log^{3}T}{(1-\gamma)T\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}
.
If this claim were true, then
taking
K_{\beta}=K_{(1-\beta)t+1}
and
K=K_{t}
and substituting the bound (
234
) into the expression (
233
) would lead to

\displaystyle|\bm{\xi}_{t}|\leq\sqrt{\frac{16\big(\log^{3}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)T\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}\Big(\max_{(1-\beta)t\leq i<t}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}}(\bm{V}_{i})+\bm{1}\Big)}+\frac{6\big(\log^{3}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)^{2}T\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}\bm{1},

(235)

thus concluding the proof of this lemma. To finish up, it is sufficient to justify the claim (
234
), which forms the content of the remainder of this proof.

Proof of the claim (
234
).

Let us use the notation in (
66
) to express

\eta_{k}^{(K)}=(1-\eta)^{K-k}\eta
. For any fixed integer
K>0
, the following vectors

\left\{\bm{P}_{t_{k}+1}(s,a)\mid 1\leq k\leq K\right\}

are identically and independently distributed; see
Li et al., 2022c
.
We can then express the term

X_{K}\coloneqq\sum_{k=K_{\beta}}^{K}(1-\eta)^{K-k}\eta\big(\bm{P}_{t_{k}+1}(s,a)-\bm{P}(s,a)\big)\bm{V}_{t_{k}},

as follows:

X_{K}=\sum_{k=K_{\beta}}^{K}z_{k}\qquad\text{with }z_{k}\coloneqq\eta_{k}^{(K)}\big(\bm{P}_{t_{k}+1}(s,a)-\bm{P}(s,a)\big)\bm{V}_{t_{k}},

where the
z_{k}
’s satisfy

\mathbb{E}\left[z_{k}\,|\,t_{k},\cdots,t_{1},\bm{V}_{t_{k}},\cdots,\bm{V}_{t_{1}}\right]=0.

We intend to invoke the Freedman inequality
to control
X_{K}
for any
K
obeying
K\leq T
.
Similar to the synchronous counterpart, we can see that

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle\coloneqq\max_{1<k\leq K}\|z_{k}\|_{\infty}\leq\eta_{k}^{K}\big(\big\|\bm{P}_{t_{k}+1}\|_{1}+\|\bm{P}\|_{1}\big)\big\|\bm{V}_{t_{k}}\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{2\eta_{k}^{K}}{1-\gamma}\leq\frac{2\eta}{1-\gamma},

\displaystyle W

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{k=K_{\beta}}^{K}\mathsf{Var}\big(z_{k}\,|\,t_{k},\cdots,t_{1},\bm{V}_{t_{k}},\cdots,\bm{V}_{t_{1}}\big)=\gamma^{2}\sum_{k=K_{\beta}}^{K}\big(\eta_{k}^{(K)}\big)^{2}\mathsf{Var}\big((\bm{P}_{t_{k}+1}-\bm{P})\bm{V}_{t_{k}}\mid\bm{V}_{t_{k}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=K_{\beta}}^{K}\big(\eta_{k}^{(K)}\big)^{2}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}(s,a)}\big(\bm{V}_{t_{k}}\big)\leq\Big(\max_{K_{\beta}\leq k\leq K}\eta_{k}^{(K)}\Big)\bigg(\sum_{k=K_{\beta}}^{K}\eta_{k}^{(K)}\bigg)\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}(s,a)}\big(\bm{V}_{t_{k}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\eta\max_{K_{\beta}\leq k\leq K}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}(s,a)}\big(\bm{V}_{t_{k}}\big)\leq\eta\max_{t_{K_{\beta}}\leq i\leq t_{K}}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}(s,a)}\big(\bm{V}_{i}\big),

where we have made use of (
71
). In addition, we make note of a trivial upper bound on
W
as follows

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\eta}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\geq\eta\max_{t_{K_{\beta}}\leq i\leq t_{K}}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}(s,a)}\big(\bm{V}_{i}\big)\geq W_{t}.

With the preceding bounds in place, applying the Freedman inequality in Theorem
6

and taking
L=\log_{2}\frac{1}{1-\gamma}
imply that

\displaystyle|X_{K}|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{8\max\bigg\{W,\frac{\sigma^{2}}{2^{L}}\bigg\}\log\frac{4|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T^{2}\log_{2}\frac{1}{1-\gamma}}{\delta}}+\frac{8\eta}{3(1-\gamma)}\log\frac{4|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T^{2}\log_{2}\frac{1}{1-\gamma}}{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{8\eta\max\Big\{\max_{t_{K_{\beta}}\leq i\leq t_{K}}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}(s,a)}(\bm{V}_{i}),1\Big\}\log\frac{4|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T^{2}\log_{2}\frac{1}{1-\gamma}}{\delta}}+\frac{8\eta}{3(1-\gamma)}\log\frac{4|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T^{2}\log_{2}\frac{1}{1-\gamma}}{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{16\big(\log^{3}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)T\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}\Big(\max_{t_{K_{\beta}}\leq i\leq t_{K}}\mathsf{Var}_{\bm{P}(s,a)}(\bm{V}_{i})+1\Big)}+\frac{6\big(\log^{3}T\big)\big(\log\frac{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T}{\delta}\big)}{(1-\gamma)^{2}T\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}

with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|T^{2}}
, provided that
\eta\leq\frac{\log^{3}T}{(1-\gamma)T\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}
.
We can thus conclude the proof by taking the union bound over all
(s,a)\in\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}
and all
1\leq K_{\beta}\leq K\leq T
.
∎

Appendix F
Lower bound for asynchronous Q-learning (Theorem
5
)

This section establishes Theorem
5
by identifying a hard MDP instance satisfying the assumed conditions.

F.1
Construction of a hard instance and its values

Let us construct an MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{hard}}
with state space
\mathcal{S}=\{0,1,2,3\}
as follows,
which is partly inspired by the idea from
Li et al., 2022b
. We shall denote by
\mathcal{A}_{s}
the action space associated with state
s
.
The probability transition kernel and the reward function of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{hard}}
are specified as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{0}=\{1,2\},

\displaystyle P(\cdot\,|\,0,1)

\displaystyle=[1,0,0,0],

\displaystyle r(0,1)=\frac{2}{3},

(236a)

\displaystyle P(\cdot\,|\,0,2)

\displaystyle=\big[1-2(1-\gamma)(1-\mu_{0}),2(1-\gamma)\mu_{1},2(1-\gamma)\mu_{2},2(1-\gamma)\mu_{3}\big],\hskip-5.0pt

\displaystyle r(0,2)=0,

(236b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{1}=\{1,2,3\},\hskip-5.0pt

\displaystyle P(\cdot\,|\,1,1)

\displaystyle=\bigg[\frac{3}{2}(1-\gamma)\mu_{0},1-\frac{3}{2}(1-\gamma)\mu_{0},0,0\bigg],

\displaystyle r(1,1)=1,

(236c)

\displaystyle P(\cdot\,|\,1,2)

\displaystyle=\bigg[\frac{3}{2}(1-\gamma)\mu_{0},1-\frac{3}{2}(1-\gamma)\mu_{0},0,0\bigg],

\displaystyle r(1,2)=1,

(236d)

\displaystyle P(\cdot\,|\,1,3)

\displaystyle=\big[0,1-3(1-\gamma)(\mu_{2}+\mu_{3}),3(1-\gamma)\mu_{2},3(1-\gamma)\mu_{3}\big],

\displaystyle r(1,3)=0,

(236e)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{2}=\{1,2\},

\displaystyle P(\cdot\,|\,2,1)

\displaystyle=\big[2(1-\gamma)\mu_{0},0,1-2(1-\gamma)\mu_{0},0\big],

\displaystyle r(2,1)=1,

(236f)

\displaystyle P(\cdot\,|\,2,2)

\displaystyle=\big[0,2(1-\gamma)\mu_{1},1-2(1-\gamma)(\mu_{1}+\mu_{3}),2(1-\gamma)\mu_{3}\big],

\displaystyle r(2,2)=0,

(236g)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{3}=\{1,2\},

\displaystyle P(\cdot\,|\,3,1)

\displaystyle=[0,0,0,1],

\displaystyle r(3,1)=\frac{3}{4},

(236h)

\displaystyle P(\cdot\,|\,3,2)

\displaystyle=\big[2(1-\gamma)\mu_{0},2(1-\gamma)\mu_{1},2(1-\gamma)\mu_{2},1-2(1-\gamma)(1-\mu_{3})\big],\hskip-5.0pt

\displaystyle r(3,2)=0,

(236i)

where the parameter
\mu=[\mu_{0},\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{3}]\in\Delta(\mathcal{S})
is set as

\displaystyle\mu=\Big[\frac{2}{5},\,\frac{2}{5}-\frac{c_{\mu}}{\log^{2}T},\,\frac{1}{5},\,\frac{c_{\mu}}{\log^{2}T}\Big],

(237)

for some sufficiently small quantity
0<c_{\mu}=O(1)
. In particular, actions
1
and
2
from state
1
are identical, which will play a similar critical role as the case of synchronous Q-learning in pinpointing the “over-estimation” issue.

In addition, let the behavior policy
\pi_{\mathsf{b}}
be uniform distributions such that

\displaystyle\pi_{\mathsf{b}}(a\,|\,s)=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{A}_{s}|},\qquad\text{for all }s\in\mathcal{S}\text{ and all }a\in\mathcal{A}_{s}.

Then the transition probability from state
s
to state
s^{\prime}
under the behavior policy
\pi_{\mathsf{b}}
is given by

\displaystyle P^{\pi_{\mathsf{b}}}(s^{\prime}\,|\,s)=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}\pi_{\mathsf{b}}(a\,|\,s)P(s^{\prime}\,|\,s,a)=\gamma\mathds{1}(s^{\prime}=s)+(1-\gamma)\mu(s^{\prime}).

With this in mind, it can be easily verified that the stationary distribution under
\pi_{\mathsf{b}}
is given by

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathsf{b}}(s,a)=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{A}_{s}|}\mu(s),\qquad\text{for all }s\in\mathcal{S}\text{ and all }a\in\mathcal{A}_{s}.

This together with (
237
) implies that

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathsf{min}}=\frac{c_{\mu}}{3\log^{2}T}.

(238)

Moreover, if the sample trajectory is initialized with an initial state distribution
\mu_{0}
,
then the marginal state distribution at time
t
can be calculated as

\displaystyle\mu_{t}=(P^{\pi_{\mathsf{b}}})^{t}\mu_{0}=(\gamma I+(1-\gamma)\mu 1^{\top})^{t}\mu_{0}=\gamma^{t}\mu_{0}+(1-\gamma^{t})\mu,

thus indicating that the total variation distance
d_{\mathsf{TV}}
between
\mu_{t}
and
\mu
obeys

\displaystyle d_{\mathsf{TV}}(\mu_{t},\mu)=\frac{1}{2}\|\mu_{t}-\mu\|_{1}=\frac{1}{2}\gamma^{t}\|\mu_{0}-\mu\|_{1}.

Consequently,
the mixing time of the sample trajectory
(
Paulin, 2015
)
obeys

t_{\mathsf{mix}}\in\Big[\frac{c_{\mathsf{mix},1}}{1-\gamma},\frac{c_{\mathsf{mix},2}}{1-\gamma}\Big]

(239)

for some universal constants
c_{\mathsf{mix},1},c_{\mathsf{mix},2}>0
.

Before embarking on the analysis of the behavior of asynchronous Q-learning,
let us first look at the optimal value function and Q-function of the constructed MDP.

Lemma 10
.

Consider the MDP
\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{hard}}
as constructed in (
236
). It holds that

\displaystyle V^{\star}(0)

\displaystyle=Q^{\star}(0,1)=\frac{2}{3(1-\gamma)};

(240a)

\displaystyle Q^{\star}(0,2)

\displaystyle<V^{\star}(0)-\frac{4}{15};

(240b)

\displaystyle V^{\star}(1)

\displaystyle=Q^{\star}(1,1)=Q^{\star}(1,2)=\frac{5+2\gamma}{(5+3\gamma)(1-\gamma)};

(240c)

\displaystyle Q^{\star}(1,3)

\displaystyle<V^{\star}(1)-\frac{7}{8};

(240d)

\displaystyle V^{\star}(2)

\displaystyle=Q^{\star}(2,1)=\frac{15+8\gamma}{3(5+4\gamma)(1-\gamma)};

(240e)

\displaystyle Q^{\star}(2,2)

\displaystyle<V^{\star}(2)-\frac{7}{9};

(240f)

\displaystyle V^{\star}(3)

\displaystyle=Q^{\star}(3,1)=\frac{3}{4(1-\gamma)};

(240g)

\displaystyle Q^{\star}(3,2)

\displaystyle<V^{\star}(3)-\frac{1}{4}.

(240h)

Proof.

In order to justify (
240
),
let us begin by defining two vectors
\bm{V}
and
\bm{Q}
as follows:

\displaystyle\bm{V}

\displaystyle=\Big[\frac{2}{3(1-\gamma)},\frac{5+2\gamma}{(5+3\gamma)(1-\gamma)},\frac{15+8\gamma}{3(5+4\gamma)(1-\gamma)},\frac{3}{4(1-\gamma)}\Big]^{\top},

\displaystyle\bm{Q}

\displaystyle=\bm{r}+\gamma\bm{P}\bm{V},

where
\bm{r}=[r(s,a)]
denotes the reward vector.
Then the claimed expressions of the value function in (
240
)
are valid as long as we can validate
V(s)=\max_{a}Q(s,a)
(namely, they satisfy the Bellman equation).
These are elementary calculations, which we omit for brevity.
Once the expressions of both the value function and the Q-function are settled,
the remaining set of advertised inequalities can be validated straightforwardly,
which is omitted as well for conciseness.
∎

F.2
Analysis for the constructed MDP

We now proceed to analyze the dynamics of asynchronous Q-learning when applied to the above MDP instance,
for which we divide into three cases based on the magnitudes of the learning rates.
Throughout the proof,
we denote by
t_{k}(s,a)
the iteration number corresponding to the
k
-th time the state-action pair
(s,a)
is visited,
and let
N_{T}(s,a)
represent the total number of visits to
(s,a)
up to time
T
.
We shall also reuse the notation of the sample transition matrix
\bm{P}_{t}
defined in (
202
), as well as the value estimate vector
\bm{V}_{t}=[V_{t}(s)]_{s\in\mathcal{S}}
as in Appendix
E.1
.

F.2.1
Case 1: small learning rates (
\eta<\frac{1}{\mu_{\mathsf{min}}(1-\gamma)T}
)

In this case, we focus attention on analyzing state
3
.
To begin with, we claim that if
\eta\leq c_{8}\frac{(1-\gamma)^{2}}{\log T}
for some sufficiently small constant
c_{8}>0
,
then one has, for all
t\leq T
,

\displaystyle Q_{t}(3,1)\leq V^{\star}(3)\qquad\text{and}\qquad Q_{t}(3,2)<V^{\star}(3)-\frac{1}{6}.

(241)

Given the assumption
\mu_{\mathsf{min}}T\geq\frac{c_{3}\log T}{(1-\gamma)^{4}}
,
the regime
\eta<\frac{1}{\mu_{\mathsf{min}}(1-\gamma)T}
clearly satisfies
\eta\leq c_{8}\frac{(1-\gamma)^{2}}{\log T}
.

Proof of Claim (
241
).

We would like to prove the second inequality in (
241
) by induction.
Clearly, it suffices to look at those iterations where the value of
Q_{t}(3,2)
changes, namely,
\{t_{k}(3,2)\}_{k\geq 1}
.
Assume for the moment that (
241
) holds for all
t<t_{k}(3,2)
.
Taking
\overline{\bm{V}}\coloneqq\big[\frac{1}{1-\gamma},\frac{1}{1-\gamma},\frac{1}{1-\gamma},V^{\star}(3)\big]^{\top}
,
we can invoke the asynchronous Q-learning update rule iteratively to deduce that

\displaystyle Q_{t_{k}(3,2)}(3,2)

\displaystyle=(1-\eta)^{k}Q_{0}(3,2)+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\gamma\eta(1-\eta)^{k-i}\bm{P}_{t_{i}(3,2)}\big((3,2),\cdot\big)\bm{V}_{t_{i}-1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\gamma\eta(1-\eta)^{k-i}\bm{P}_{t_{i}(3,2)}\big((3,2),\cdot\big)\overline{\bm{V}}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\bm{P}(\cdot\,|\,3,2)\overline{\bm{V}}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\gamma\eta(1-\eta)^{k-i}\big[\bm{P}_{t_{i}(3,2)}\big((3,2),\cdot\big)-\bm{P}(\cdot\,|\,3,2)\big]\overline{\bm{V}}

\displaystyle<\gamma\bm{P}(\cdot\,|\,3,2)\overline{\bm{V}}+\frac{1}{12}

\displaystyle\leq V^{\star}(3)-\frac{1}{6}.

(242)

Here, the second line holds since
\overline{\bm{V}}
serves as an entrywise upper bound on
\bm{V}_{t}
;
the third line follows since
\sum_{i=1}^{k}\eta(1-\eta)^{k-i}\leq 1
;
the fourth line in (
242
) is valid since,
according to the Bernstein inequality (see
Li et al., 2022c
),

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{i=1}^{k}\eta(1-\eta)^{k-i}\big[\bm{P}_{t_{i}(3,2)}\big((3,2),\cdot\big)-\bm{P}(\cdot\,|\,3,2)\big]\overline{\bm{V}}\bigg|<\frac{1}{12}

holds with probability at least
1-1/T
;
and the validity of the last inequality can be shown by observing that

\displaystyle\gamma\bm{P}(\cdot\,|\,3,2)\overline{\bm{V}}

\displaystyle=\gamma P(3\,|\,3,2)\overline{V}(3)+\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\big(1-P(3\,|\,3,2)\big)

\displaystyle=\gamma\Big(1-2(1-\gamma)\Big)\overline{V}(3)+\gamma\big(2(1-\gamma)\mu_{3}\big)\overline{V}(3)+\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\big(2(1-\gamma)\mu_{3}\big)

\displaystyle=\gamma\Big(1-2(1-\gamma)\Big)\overline{V}(3)+3.5\gamma\mu_{3}\leq\gamma\big(1-2(1-\gamma)\big)\overline{V}(3)+2\gamma

\displaystyle=\overline{V}(3)-\frac{3}{4}(2\gamma+1)+2\gamma\leq\overline{V}(3)-\frac{1}{4},

where we have used the facts that
\overline{V}(3)=V^{\star}(3)=\frac{3}{4(1-\gamma)}
,

P(3\,|\,3,2)=1-2(1-\gamma)(1-\mu_{3})

and
\gamma<1
.
Thus, standard induction arguments immediately validate the second inequality in the claim (
241
) for all
t\leq T
.

Regarding the first inequality of (
241
), it is seen that for any
k\geq 1
,

\displaystyle Q_{t_{k}(3,1)}(3,1)

\displaystyle=(1-\eta)^{k}Q_{0}(3,1)+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\eta(1-\eta)^{k-i}\Big(r(3,1)+\gamma\bm{P}_{t_{i}(3,1)}\big((3,1),\cdot\big)\bm{V}_{t_{i}(3,1)-1}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\eta(1-\eta)^{k-i}\big(r(3,1)+\gamma\overline{V}(3)\big)\leq V^{\star}(3),

where we have used the facts that
Q_{0}\equiv 0
and
\overline{V}(3)=V^{\star}(3)=\frac{3}{4(1-\gamma)}

and the elementary inequality
\sum_{i=1}^{k}\eta(1-\eta)^{k-i}\leq 1
.
Given that the above bound holds for all
k\geq 1
(and
\{t_{k}(3,1)\}_{k\geq 1}
correspond to all iterations when the value of
Q_{t}(3,1)
changes), we have established the first advertised inequality in (
241
).
∎

Next, let us define

\displaystyle s\coloneqq\max\Big\{k:Q_{t_{k}(3,1)}(3,1)<V^{\star}(3)-\frac{1}{8}\Big\}.

(243)

From this definition and Claim (
241
), we know that for any
k>s
, it holds that

Q_{t_{k}(3,1)}(3,1)\geq V^{\star}(3)-\frac{1}{8}.

Recognizing that

\displaystyle\big|Q_{t_{k}(s,a)}(s,a)-Q_{t_{k-1}(s,a)}(s,a)\big|=\eta\big|Q_{t_{k}(s,a)}(s,a)-r(s,a)-\gamma\bm{P}_{t_{k}(s,a)}\big((s,a),\cdot\big)\bm{V}_{t_{k-1}(s,a)}\big|\leq\frac{\eta}{1-\gamma}<\frac{1}{24},

we can readily obtain

Q_{t_{k-1}(3,1)}(3,1)\geq Q_{t_{k}(3,1)}(3,1)-\frac{1}{24}\geq V^{\star}(3)-\frac{1}{6}>Q_{t_{k-1}(3,1)}(3,2),

\Longrightarrow\qquad V_{t_{k-1}(3,1)}(3)=Q_{t_{k-1}(3,1)}(3,1),\qquad\forall k>s.

Combine this with the Q-learning update rule to arrive at

\displaystyle Q_{t_{k}(3,1)}(3,1)

\displaystyle=(1-\eta)Q_{t_{k-1}(3,1)}(3,1)+\eta\big(r(3,1)+\gamma Q_{t_{k-1}(3,1)}(3,1)\big)

\displaystyle=\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)\big)Q_{t_{k-1}(3,1)}(3,1)+\frac{3}{4}\eta

\displaystyle=\cdots=\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)\big)^{k-s}Q_{t_{s}(3,1)}(3,1)+\frac{3}{4}\eta\sum_{i=0}^{k-s-1}\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)\big)^{i}

\displaystyle=V^{\star}(3)-\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)\big)^{k-s}\big[V^{\star}(3)-Q_{t_{s}(3,1)}(3,1)\big],

where the last inequality holds since
V^{\star}(3)=\frac{3}{4(1-\gamma)}
.
This taken together with (
243
) (so that
V^{\star}(3)-Q_{t_{s}(3,1)}(3,1)\geq 1/8
) leads to

\displaystyle V^{\star}(3)-Q_{T}(3,1)=Q^{\star}(3,1)-Q_{T}(3,1)\geq\frac{1}{8}\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)\big)^{N_{T}(3,1)}\geq c_{9}

(244)

for some constant
c_{9}>0
;
here, the last inequality holds since, according to
Li et al., 2022c
,

\displaystyle N_{T}(3,1)\leq\frac{3}{2}\mu_{\mathsf{b}}(3,1)T=\frac{9}{4}\mu_{\mathsf{min}}T\leq O\Big(\frac{1}{\eta(1-\gamma)}\Big)

occurs with probability at least
1-1/T
, provided that

T\geq\frac{443t_{\mathsf{mix}}\log(10T)}{\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}
.
Note that according to (
238
) and (
239
),

\frac{t_{\mathsf{mix}}}{\mu_{\mathsf{min}}}
is on the order of
\frac{\log^{2}T}{c_{\mu}(1-\gamma)}
.

Putting (
241
) and (
244
) together then reveals that
with probability at least
1-2/T
,

\max_{a}\big|Q^{\star}(3,a)-Q_{T}(3,a)\big|\geq V^{\star}(3)-V_{T}(3)=V^{\star}(3)-\max\big\{Q_{T}(3,1),Q_{T}(3,2)\big\}\geq\min\Big\{\frac{1}{6},c_{9}\Big\}.

(245)

F.2.2
Case 2: large learning rates (
\eta>\frac{\log T}{\mu_{\mathsf{min}}(1-\gamma)^{2}T}
)

Case 2.1:
\eta\geq\frac{c_{8}(1-\gamma)^{2}}{\log T}
for some small enough constant
c_{8}>0
.

Under the condition that
\eta\geq\frac{c_{8}(1-\gamma)^{2}}{\log T}
,
we claim that with probability at least
\frac{1-\gamma}{50}
,

\exists(s,a)\qquad\text{s.t.}\quad\big|Q_{T}(s,a)-Q^{\star}(s,a)\big|\geq c_{10}\frac{1-\gamma}{\log T}

(246)

for some universal constant
c_{10}>0
.
We shall first prove this claim.

Proof of Claim (
246
).

We shall focus attention on the case where
(s_{T-1},a_{T-1})=(2,1)
.
Given that the stationary distribution obeys
\mu_{\mathsf{b}}(2,1)=1/10
and that
T
is sufficiently large (so that the empirical distribution approaches the stationary distribution),
we know that

\mathbb{P}\big((s_{T-1},a_{T-1})=(2,1)\big)\geq\mu_{\mathsf{b}}(2,1)/2=1/20.

Let us first look at the case where
\big|V_{T-1}(0)-V^{\star}(0)\big|>\frac{1}{27(1-\gamma)}
. It follows from
P(\cdot\,|\,0,1)=[1,0,0,0]
that

\big|Q_{T}(0,1)-Q^{\star}(0,1)\big|=\big|r(0,1)+\gamma V_{T-1}(0)-V^{\star}(0)\big|=\gamma\big|V_{T-1}(0)-V^{\star}(0)\big|>\frac{0.03}{1-\gamma}

as long as
\gamma\geq 0.95
,
which clearly satisfies (
246
).
When it comes to the complement case where
\big|V_{T-1}(0)-V^{\star}(0)\big|\leq\frac{1}{27(1-\gamma)}
,
either of the following two scenarios will happen:

•

If
|V_{T-1}(2)-V_{T-1}(0)|\leq\frac{1}{27(1-\gamma)}
, then the assumption
\big|V_{T-1}(0)-V^{\star}(0)\big|\leq\frac{1}{27(1-\gamma)}
yields

\displaystyle Q_{T}(2,2)

\displaystyle\leq V_{T-1}(2)\leq V_{T-1}(0)+\frac{1}{27(1-\gamma)}\leq V^{\star}(0)+\frac{2}{27(1-\gamma)}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\big[1-2(1-\gamma)(\mu_{1}+\mu_{3})\big]V^{\star}(2)-\frac{1}{27(1-\gamma)}<Q^{\star}(2,2)-\frac{1}{27(1-\gamma)},

(247)

where the second line holds since, for
\gamma\geq 0.95
,

\displaystyle\gamma\big[1-2(1-\gamma)(\mu_{1}+\mu_{3})\big]V^{\star}(2)

\displaystyle=\gamma\Big[1-\frac{4}{5}(1-\gamma)\Big]\frac{15+8\gamma}{3(5+4\gamma)(1-\gamma)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{7}{9(1-\gamma)}=V^{\star}(0)+\frac{1}{9(1-\gamma)},

and the last inequality holds since
Q^{\star}(2,2)>r(2,2)+\gamma P(2\,|\,2,2)V^{\star}(2)
(from the Bellman equation).

•

Consider instead the scenario with
|V_{T-1}(2)-V_{T-1}(0)|>\frac{1}{27(1-\gamma)}
.
For notational convenience, define

Q_{T}^{s}(2,1)\coloneqq(1-\eta)Q_{T-1}(2,1)+\eta\big(r(2,1)+\gamma V_{T-1}(s)\big),\quad\forall s\in\mathcal{S}.

Recognizing that
\min\big\{P(0\,|\,2,1),P(2\,|\,2,1)\big\}\geq 2(1-\gamma)\mu_{0}
,
we can show that
with probability at least
(1-\gamma)\mu_{0}=\frac{2(1-\gamma)}{5}
,

\displaystyle\big|Q_{T}(2,1)-Q^{\star}(2,1)\big|

\displaystyle=\max\Big\{\big|Q_{T}^{0}(2,1)-Q^{\star}(2,1)\big|,\,\big|Q_{T}^{2}(2,1)-Q^{\star}(2,1)\big|\Big\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\big|Q_{T}^{0}(2,1)-Q_{T}^{2}(2,1)\big|=\frac{1}{2}\eta\gamma\big|V_{T-1}(0)-V_{T-1}(2)\big|

\displaystyle>\frac{\eta\gamma}{54(1-\gamma)}\geq\frac{0.95c_{8}(1-\gamma)}{54\log T},

where the last inequality holds since
\eta\geq\frac{c_{8}(1-\gamma)^{2}}{\log T}
and
\gamma\geq 0.95
.

We can thus conclude that for all the above scenarios, the claim (
246
) holds with probability at least

\frac{1-\gamma}{50}
.
∎

Case 2.2:
\frac{\log T}{\mu_{\mathsf{min}}(1-\gamma)^{2}T}<\eta\leq\frac{c_{8}(1-\gamma)^{2}}{\log T}
.

Recall from Claim (
241
) that: when
\eta\leq\frac{c_{8}(1-\gamma)^{2}}{\log T}
, one has

\displaystyle Q_{t}(3,1)\leq V^{\star}(3)\qquad\text{and}\qquad Q_{t}(3,2)\leq V^{\star}(3)-\frac{1}{6},\qquad\forall t\leq T.

(248)

In addition, for any
k\geq\frac{\log T}{\eta(1-\gamma)}
, we can use
P(\cdot\,|\,3,1)=[0,0,0,1]
and the Bellman equation to derive

\displaystyle Q_{t_{k}(3,1)}(3,1)

\displaystyle=(1-\eta)Q_{t_{k-1}(3,1)}(3,1)+\eta\big(r(3,1)+\gamma V_{t_{k}(3,1)-1}(3)\big)

\displaystyle\geq(1-\eta)Q_{t_{k-1}(3,1)}(3,1)+\eta\Big(\frac{3}{4}+\gamma Q_{t_{k-1}(3,1)}(3,1)\Big)

\displaystyle=\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)\big)Q_{t_{k-1}}(3,1)+\frac{3}{4}\eta

\displaystyle=\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)\big)^{k}Q_{0}(3,1)+\frac{3}{4}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\eta\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)\big)^{k-i}

\displaystyle=\Big[1-\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)\big)^{k}\Big]V^{\star}(3)\geq V^{\star}(3)-\frac{1}{T(1-\gamma)},

where the last line is valid since
V^{\star}(3)=\frac{3}{4(1-\gamma)}
and
Q_{0}(3,1)=0
.
It is also seen from
Li et al., 2022c
that, with probability at least
1-1/T
,

\displaystyle N_{T/3}(3,1)\geq\frac{1}{2}\mu_{\mathsf{b}}(3,1)T=\frac{3}{4}\mu_{\mathsf{min}}T\geq\frac{\log T}{\eta(1-\gamma)}

as long as
\eta\geq\frac{\log T}{\mu_{\mathsf{min}}(1-\gamma)T}
.
The above two results taken collectively yield

\displaystyle 0\leq V^{\star}(3)-V_{t}(3)\leq V^{\star}(3)-Q_{t}(3,1)\leq\frac{1}{T(1-\gamma)},\qquad\text{for all }\frac{T}{3}\leq t\leq T.

(249)

Next, we move on to analyze state
2
.
Towards this end, let us define

\displaystyle s\coloneqq\max\Big\{k:Q_{t_{k}(2,1)}(2,2)>Q_{t_{k}(2,1)}(2,1)-\frac{1}{2}\penalty\ \penalty\ \text{or}\penalty\ \penalty\ \big|Q_{t_{k}(2,1)}(2,1)-V^{\star}(2)\big|>\frac{1}{4}\Big\}.

(250)

Note that when
\eta\leq\frac{c_{8}(1-\gamma)^{2}}{\log T}
, one has

\displaystyle\big|Q_{t_{k}(s,a)}(s,a)-Q_{t_{k-1}(s,a)}(s,a)\big|=\eta\big|Q_{t_{k}(s,a)}(s,a)-r(s,a)-\gamma\bm{P}_{t_{k}(s,a)}\big((s,a),\cdot\big)\bm{V}_{t_{k}(s,a)-1}\big|\leq\frac{\eta}{1-\gamma}<\frac{1}{4},

which combined with (
250
) implies that
Q_{t_{k-1}(2,1)}(2,2)<Q_{t_{k-1}(2,1)}(2,1)
for any
k>s
and hence

\displaystyle V_{t_{k-1}(2,1)}(2)=Q_{t_{k-1}(2,1)}(2,1)\qquad\text{ for }k>s.

(251)

This crucial identity together with the construction of
P(\cdot\,|\,2,1)
in turn allows one to derive, for any
k>s
,

\displaystyle Q_{t_{k}(2,1)}(2,1)

\displaystyle=(1-\eta)Q_{t_{k-1}(2,1)}(2,1)+\eta\Big(r(2,1)+\gamma\bm{P}_{t_{k}(2,1)}\big((2,1),\cdot\big)\bm{V}_{t_{k}(2,1)-1}\Big)

\displaystyle=\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)(1+2\gamma\mu_{0})\big)Q_{t_{k-1}(2,1)}(2,1)

\displaystyle\qquad+\eta\Big(1+2\gamma(1-\gamma)\mu_{0}V_{t_{k}(2,1)-1}(0)+\gamma\Big(\bm{P}_{t_{k}(2,1)}\big((2,1),\cdot\big)-\bm{P}(\cdot\,|\,2,1)\Big)\bm{V}_{t_{k}(2,1)-1}\Big)

\displaystyle=\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)(1+2\gamma\mu_{0})\big)^{k-s}Q_{t_{s}(2,1)}(2,1)+\eta\sum_{i=s+1}^{k}\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)(1+2\gamma\mu_{0})\big)^{k-i}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\Big(1+2\gamma(1-\gamma)\mu_{0}V_{t_{i}(2,1)-1}(0)+\gamma\Big(\bm{P}_{t_{i}(2,1)}\big((2,1),\cdot\big)-\bm{P}(\cdot\,|\,2,1)\Big)\bm{V}_{t_{i}(2,1)-1}\Big),

(252)

thus leading to

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[Q_{t_{k}(2,1)}(2,1)\mid Q_{t_{s}(2,1)}(2,1)\big]

\displaystyle\leq\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)(1+2\gamma\mu_{0})\big)^{k-s}Q_{t_{s}(2,1)}(2,1)

\displaystyle\qquad+\eta\sum_{i=s+1}^{k}\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)(1+2\gamma\mu_{0})\big)^{k-i}\big(1+2\gamma(1-\gamma)\mu_{0}V^{\star}(0)\big)

\displaystyle\leq\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)(1+2\gamma\mu_{0})\big)^{k-s}Q_{t_{s}(2,1)}(2,1)

\displaystyle\qquad+\eta\sum_{i=s+1}^{k}\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)(1+2\gamma\mu_{0})\big)^{k-i}V^{\star}(2)

\displaystyle=V^{\star}(2)-\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)(1+2\gamma\mu_{0})\big)^{k-s}\big(V^{\star}(2)-Q_{t_{s}(2,1)}(2,1)\big)

\displaystyle\leq V^{\star}(2)-\frac{1}{4}\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)(1+2\gamma\mu_{0})\big)^{k-s},

where the second inequality arises from the Bellman equation (so that
V^{\star}(2)\geq 1+2\gamma(1-\gamma)\mu_{0}V^{\star}(0)
).

•

If
\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)(1+2\gamma\mu_{0})\big)^{k-s}\geq\frac{1}{2}
, then we can readily see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|Q_{T}(2,1)-Q^{\star}(2,1)\big|^{2}\Big]\geq\Big(\mathbb{E}\big[Q_{t_{k}(2,1)}(2,1)-V^{\star}(2)\mid Q_{t_{s}(2,1)}(2,1)\big]\Big)^{2}\geq\frac{1}{64}.

(253)

•

Otherwise, consider the case where
\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)(1+2\gamma\mu_{0})\big)^{k-s}<\frac{1}{2}
.
The expression (
252
) allows one to control the variance as follows:

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}\big(Q_{t_{k}(2,1)}(2,1)\mid Q_{t_{s}(2,1)}(2,1)\big)=\eta^{2}\gamma^{2}\sum_{i=s+1}^{k}\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)(1+2\gamma\mu_{0})\big)^{2(k-i)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\Big(\bm{P}_{t_{i}(2,1)}\big((2,1),\cdot\big)-\bm{P}(\cdot\,|\,2,1)\Big)\bm{V}_{t_{i}(2,1)-1}\Big)^{2}\mid\bm{V}_{t_{i}(2,1)-1}\bigg]\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=\eta^{2}\gamma^{2}\sum_{i=s+1}^{k}\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)(1+2\gamma\mu_{0})\big)^{2(k-i)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\bigg[2(1-\gamma)\mu_{0}\big(1-2(1-\gamma)\mu_{0}\big)\Big\{V_{t_{i}(2,1)-1}(2)-V_{t_{i}(2,1)-1}(0)\Big\}^{2}\mid\bm{V}_{t_{i}(2,1)-1}\bigg]\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\eta^{2}\gamma^{2}\sum_{i=s+1}^{k}\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)(1+2\gamma\mu_{0})\big)^{2(k-i)}\left\{(1-\gamma)\mu_{0}\cdot\frac{1}{36(1-\gamma)^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{\eta^{2}\gamma^{2}\mu_{0}}{36(1-\gamma)}\frac{1-\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)(1+2\gamma\mu_{0})\big)^{k-s}}{\eta(1-\gamma)(1+2\gamma\mu_{0})}\geq\frac{\eta}{200(1+2\gamma\mu_{0})(1-\gamma)^{2}}\geq\frac{\eta}{400(1-\gamma)^{2}},

where the third inequality holds since

V_{t_{i}(2,1)-1}(2)-V_{t_{i}(2,1)-1}(0)\geq V^{\star}(2)-\frac{1}{4}-V^{\star}(0)>\frac{5}{27(1-\gamma)}
(using the definition of
s
in (
250
)),
and the last line uses
\gamma\geq 0.95
and
\mu_{0}=2/5
.
As a result,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|Q_{T}(2,1)-Q^{\star}(2,1)\big|^{2}\Big]\geq\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathsf{Var}\big(Q_{t_{k}(2,1)}(2,1)\mid Q_{t_{s}(2,1)}(2,1)\big)\Big]\geq\frac{\eta}{400(1-\gamma)^{2}}.

(254)

Taking the above two results together reveals that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|Q_{T}(2,1)-Q^{\star}(2,1)\big|^{2}\Big]\geq\min\bigg\{\frac{\eta}{400(1-\gamma)^{2}},\frac{1}{64}\bigg\}\geq\min\bigg\{\frac{\log T}{400\mu_{\mathsf{min}}(1-\gamma)^{4}T},\frac{1}{64}\bigg\}.

(255)

Combining Case 2.1 and Case 2.2.

Putting together (
246
) and (
255
) together leads to

\max_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|Q_{T}(s,a)-Q^{\star}(s,a)\big|^{2}\Big]\geq\min\bigg\{\frac{\log T}{400\mu_{\mathsf{min}}(1-\gamma)^{4}T},\frac{1}{64},\frac{c_{10}^{2}(1-\gamma)^{3}}{50\log^{2}T}\bigg\},

(256)

provided that
\eta>\frac{\log T}{\mu_{\mathsf{min}}(1-\gamma)^{2}T}
.

F.2.3
Case 3: medium learning rates (
\frac{1}{\mu_{\mathsf{min}}(1-\gamma)T}\leq\eta\leq\frac{\log T}{\mu_{\mathsf{min}}(1-\gamma)^{2}T}
)

We now shift attention to the dynamics underlying state
1
, and look at
its associated value function
V_{t}(1)
.

Two auxiliary sequences.

Before proceeding, we abuse notation by defining

\displaystyle t_{i}\coloneqq\min\Big\{t>t_{i-1}:\text{both pairs $(1,1)$ and $(1,2)$ have been visited during $(t_{i-1},t]$}\Big\},

and letting
N_{(t_{k-1},t_{k}]}(1,a,1)
(resp.
N_{(t_{k-1},t_{k}]}(1,a)
) denote the number of times the sample trajectory
visits
(s,a,s^{\prime})=(1,a,1)
(resp.
(s,a)=(1,a)
)
within the time interval
(t_{k-1},t_{k}]
.
From standard Bernstein’s inequality (see
Li et al., 2022c
) and the definition of
t_{i}
,
one can easily see that with probability at least
1-1/T
,

\displaystyle 1\leq N_{(t_{i-1},t_{i}]}(1,a)\leq c_{11}\log T

(257)

holds simultaneously for all
t_{i}\leq T
, where
c_{11}>0
is some suitable constant.
In turn,
this bound (
257
) also implies that

K\coloneqq\max\big\{k:t_{k}\leq T\big\}\geq c_{12}\frac{T}{\log T}

(258)

for some constant
c_{12}>0
.
These follow from fairly standard concentration arguments and are hence omitted.

Under our assumption on
T
, the sample trajectory mixes well after
T/3
iterations.
In order to further remove the effect of
Q_{T/3}(1,a)
, let us introduce the following auxiliary sequence

\displaystyle\widehat{Q}_{k}(a)=(1-\eta)\widehat{Q}_{k-1}(a)+\eta\Big\{\frac{1}{3}+\gamma P_{k}(a)\widehat{V}_{k-1}\Big\},

(259)

where
\widehat{V}_{k-1}\coloneqq\max_{a}\widehat{Q}_{k-1}(a)
,

\displaystyle\widehat{Q}_{0}(a)

\displaystyle\coloneqq Q^{\star}(1,a)-V^{\star}(0)=\frac{5}{3(5+3\gamma)(1-\gamma)},\qquad a=1,2

\displaystyle P_{k}(a)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{N_{(t_{k-1},t_{k}]}(1,a,1)}{N_{(t_{k-1},t_{k}]}(1,a)}.

Repeating the proof of (
165
) (which we omit here for brevity), we arrive at the following relation:

\displaystyle Q_{t_{k}(1,a)}(1,a)-V^{\star}(0)\geq\widehat{Q}_{k}(a)-\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)\big)^{k}-\frac{1}{T(1-\gamma)^{2}},\qquad a\in\{1,2\}.

(260)

Furthermore, in order to control
\widehat{Q}_{k}(a)
, we construct an additional auxiliary sequence as follows

\displaystyle\overline{Q}_{k}=(1-\eta)\overline{Q}_{k-1}+\eta\Big\{\frac{1}{3}+\gamma P_{k}(1)\overline{Q}_{k-1}\Big\}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\overline{Q}_{0}=V^{\star}(1)-V^{\star}(0)=\frac{5}{3(5+3\gamma)(1-\gamma)}.

(261)

From the basic fact that
\widehat{V}_{k}=\max_{a}\widehat{Q}_{k}(a)\geq\widehat{Q}_{k}(1)
, it can be easily verified that

\displaystyle\widehat{Q}_{k}(1)\geq(1-\eta)\widehat{Q}_{k-1}(1)+\eta\Big\{\frac{1}{3}+\gamma P_{k}(1)\widehat{Q}_{k-1}(1)\Big\}\geq\overline{Q}_{k},

(262)

which in turn motivates us to lower bound
\widehat{V}_{k}
by controlling
\overline{Q}_{k}
.
Using similar analysis for (
168
), we reach

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big\{\widehat{V}_{k}\geq\frac{1}{5(1-\gamma)}\Big\}\geq\frac{1}{2},\qquad\text{for any }k\text{ with}\penalty\ t_{k}\geq\frac{2T}{3}.

(263)

Main proof.

With the preceding auxiliary sequences in place, let us define (akin to the synchronous case)

\displaystyle\Delta_{k}(a)

\displaystyle\coloneqq\widehat{Q}_{k}(a)+V^{\star}(0)-Q^{\star}(1,a),\qquad a=1,2;

(264a)

\displaystyle\Delta_{k,\mathsf{max}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\max_{a}\Delta_{k}(a).

(264b)

Based on the iterative update rule (
259
), we can once again derive

\displaystyle\Delta_{k}(a)=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\eta\big(1-\eta\big)^{k-i}\gamma\Big(p\Delta_{k-1,\mathsf{max}}+\big(P_{k}(a)-p\big)\widehat{V}_{k-1}\Big),\qquad a=1,2,

(265)

where
p=1-\frac{3}{2}(1-\gamma)\mu_{0}
.
Then adopting the same analysis as for the synchronous case, we arrive at

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\max_{a}\big|Q_{T}(1,a)-Q^{\star}(1,a)\big|\Big]\geq\mathbb{E}\big[\big|V_{T}(1)-V^{\star}(1)\big|\big]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{13}}{\sqrt{\mu_{\mathsf{min}}(1-\gamma)^{4}T\log^{3}T}},

(266)

for some constant
c_{13}>0
.
Here, we have made use of the fact that

\displaystyle\big(1-\eta(1-\gamma)\big)^{K}\leq\bigg(1-\frac{3\log^{2}T}{c_{\mu}T}\bigg)^{\frac{c_{12}T}{\log T}}\leq\frac{1}{T},

an immediate consequence of (
258
) and the assumption that
\eta(1-\gamma)\geq\frac{1}{\mu_{\mathsf{min}}T}=\frac{3\log^{2}T}{c_{\mu}T}
.

F.2.4
Putting all this together

Combining (
245
), (
256
), and (
266
) leads to

\max_{s,a}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|Q_{T}(s,a)-Q^{\star}(s,a)\big|^{2}\Big]\geq\min\bigg\{c_{9}^{2},\frac{\log T}{400\mu_{\mathsf{min}}(1-\gamma)^{4}T},\frac{1}{64},\frac{c_{10}^{2}(1-\gamma)^{3}}{\log^{2}T},\frac{c_{13}^{2}}{\mu_{\mathsf{min}}(1-\gamma)^{4}T\log^{3}T}\bigg\},

(267)

for any
0<\eta<1
.
Then the conclusion is handy under the proviso that
T\geq\frac{c_{3}}{\mu_{\mathsf{min}}(1-\gamma)^{7}\log T}
.
